# Verdicts: how a reckoning's readings are combined

Sigla, numbering and quoting rules are in [README.md](README.md).

The Reckonings (SPEC §10.11) show each section of a reckoning, its pebbles
and its reading, one by one. The owner (2026-10-20): the readings must
also be combined and general conclusions drawn, for the birth, for the
marriage match and for the other reckonings. This file gathers what WB and
its readers say about how that is done, and plans the readings that will
settle it (ROADMAP *Readings*, RD18–RD28; the build is R4, block 58).

Two of the owner's rules frame it. **The sources decide** (2026-10-09): if
WB gives no weight, none is invented; if it gives a rule, the rule is
built. **Read for action** (Y1, 2026-10-16): a conclusion names the points
to weigh and the practices that counterbalance them, never a dooming
verdict, and gives every remedy its source names.

**State, 2026-10-20.** Planned. The passages under *Found 2026-10-20* are
BDRC's etext of WB (IE0OPI51524892), searched for the words of counting
and summing; **none of them is read on the scan yet**, and the etext's
OCR errors are kept as they stand. The rest cites passages already read in
the other topic files.

## The method in WB: five steps

WB never gives one score for a whole reckoning (the reckoning for the dead
has none, [reckonings.md](reckonings.md), *Searched and not found*; the
departure weighs none of its seventeen parts against another,
[marriage.md](marriage.md)). What it gives is a ladder, and every
reckoning climbs some of it:

| Step | What is combined | WB's rule | Read so far |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Two elements meeting | The colour of a pebble: mother three white, friend two, son white and black, enemy two black, the same element white for earth and water, black for the rest (vol. 1, p. 380; p. 417; vol. 2, p. 3) | On the scan; built (SPEC §5.9) |
| 2 | The pebbles of one aspect or one starting point | The verdict (ཁ་དམར, kha dmar), also the deciding pebble (བཏང་གཅོད, btang gcod): count the white and black that lie there, not the threes and twos, and lay the verdict on the more; even, a speckled one (ཁྲ་བོ, khra bo) | Vol. 1, p. 381 and p. 261 on the scan; vol. 2, p. 46 on the Gser thang reprint ([year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)); the notes' p. 490 etext only (below). Built for the year of age with "even, none" (open question 167) |
| 3 | The verdicts read as a set | All four aspects white or black (vol. 1, pp. 384–385, on the scan, built); the four mothers, sons and friends of the birth, and many white or many black over the four aspects (p. 338, etext); the household's verdicts gathered (pp. 385, 407); the reckoning for the dead's thirteenth point, whose root pebbles are the verdicts of the twelve before it (vol. 2, p. 50) | In part |
| 4 | The set placed in a life | Whose aspect matters most (vol. 1, p. 242; pp. 415–416, on the scan, built in Y1); which part of life a sign rules: the year the first, the month the next, the day and hour the last (p. 261, on the scan); the upper pebbles the forebears and the first half of life, the lower oneself and the second (p. 471, etext); each aspect's domain: the life force the span of life, the body illness, the power wealth, the luck merit, the mewa gods and demons (vol. 2, p. 22, etext) | In part |
| 5 | The whole reckoning | Not judged by one or two results: the force and number of the good and the bad weighed throughout, good if the good are greater and more, bad if the bad, middling if even, and the rites can turn the bad (vol. 1, p. 349, etext, the close of the birth); a kind or class the whole falls into (the birth's twenty-one children, pp. 338–342; the match's spouse of karma, of kin or of enmity, p. 351, with the notes' "look at good and bad in general", vol. 2, p. 524); the illness by the element that is most (p. 435; the notes, p. 497); then the remedies, sized (vol. 2, p. 50) and by aspect (vol. 2, p. 22) | Etext only |

WB also warns against the shortcuts: at the end of the illness chapter
(vol. 1, p. 452, etext) it mocks those who marvel when one or two results
fit, and those who say only a rough good or bad and then lay down whatever
rite comes to hand, "verdict-makers by guesswork" («ཧ་ལམ་གྱི་ཁ་དམར་མཁན»), who do
not know the treatise.

## Reckoning by reckoning

What each reckoning gives for steps 2–5, what the app shows, and the
reading that settles the rest.

- **The year of age** (ch. 24; SPEC §5.9.1). Steps 2–4 are built: the
  predictive pebble of each aspect, all four alike, whose aspect matters
  most. Left: the tie (open question 167), "the four classes' verdicts
  gathered" (p. 385, «སྡེ་བཞིའི་ཁ་དམར་བསྡུས», etext) and the household's
  verdicts (pp. 385–387, 407, read on the scan, not built: they need the
  household). RD18, RD20.
- **The birth** (ch. 22; SPEC §5.9.2). Built: the sections one by one.
  Not built: the hundred pebbles and the twenty decisive ones (pp. 259–262,
  read), their readings (pp. 328–338), the sets read whole (p. 338), the
  twenty-one children of the secret *yang tig*, the innermost essence
  (ཡང་ཏིག, yang tig; pp. 338–342), and the chapter's rule for the whole
  (p. 349). The birth's conclusion is these, in this order. RD19.
- **The marriage match** (ch. 23; SPEC §5.9.3). Built: the points one by
  one. WB names the match's conclusion at its start (p. 351): with the
  fifteen points "the spouse of karma, of kin or of enmity, and the
  general sum of the mansions' results are known". Inside the points, two
  counts decide: point 7, whichever of the two has more pebbles wins
  (p. 359, read; open question 166), and point 13's rise and decline, more
  white agree, fewer disagree (p. 364, etext). The notes (vol. 2,
  pp. 523–526, etext) say how: the bad years are reckoned in general, not
  pair by pair; each aspect's kind of spouse is joined and then the good
  and bad looked at in general; point 7's five counts are summed for each
  spouse, the two compared, and the two together odd disagree, even agree;
  the bride's ill words are read as the reckoning's faults, "the result of
  karma" being "the reckoning bad in general". RD21.
- **The illness reckoning** (ch. 25; H2, not built). Its conclusion is the
  illness itself: the element that is most among the year, month, day,
  hour and life force (p. 435), in shares by the notes (two wood, two fire
  and three iron of seven, iron's the illness, p. 497 on the scan; RD22),
  then its door, its
  signs, its place and its rite ([health.md](health.md)). RD22.
- **The reckoning for the dead** (ch. 29; not built). Its thirteenth point
  gathers the verdicts of the twelve, white where white, black where black,
  even a pair, and from them the size of the rites (vol. 2, p. 50, the
  sizes read on the scan, [reckonings.md](reckonings.md)). RD23.
- **The thirty starting points, the thirteen circles and the small
  reckonings** (ch. 26–27; not built). Each starting point has its verdict
  "examined with intelligence" (vol. 1, p. 471, etext); the small
  reckonings of ch. 27 count twenty-five pebbles for each sign, lay five
  verdicts, and read them aspect by aspect with the aspect's remedy
  (vol. 2, pp. 22–23, etext). RD23.
- **The reckoning of the moment** (ch. 30; not built): "count white and
  black and give the verdict" (vol. 2, p. 172, on the scan,
  [reckonings.md](reckonings.md)). RD23.
- **Between the reckonings.** Whether WB reads the birth, the year of age
  and the health divination of the month, day and hour together, and which
  stands over which, is not found yet: the year of age already takes the
  natal mewa and trigram among its signs (SPEC §5.9.1), and the birth's
  p. 261 gives the parts of life by year, month, day and hour. RD24.

## Found 2026-10-20 in the etext (not read on the scan)

Page = printed page (vol. 1: image − 10; vol. 2: image − 8). The Tibetan is
the etext's, OCR errors and gaps as they stand; the English is a first
reading, to be checked.

**The birth, the sets read whole** (vol. 1, p. 338, img. 348), after the
readings of each aspect's good and bad six:

> དེ་ལ་མ་གཞི་དཀར་པོར་བྱུང་། །ཕ་མས་དར་བུ་ཡ་རབས་བརྒྱུད། ། མ་བཞི་ནག་ན་ཕ་མེས་གུད། །ཕ་དྲིན་ཆུང་ལ་རྒྱབ་བརྟེན་མིར། །བཙལ་དགོས་ཡིན་ལ། དཀར་ནག་ཕྱེད། །བྱུང་བ་ཕ་མེས་སྐྱིད་སྡུག་དང་། དར་གུད་ཆེ་ཞིང་ … །སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་བཞི་པོ་ཡི། །རྡེལ་དཀར་མང་བྱུང་མིས་ཁུར་འོང་། །བྱ་བ་གྲུབ་ཅིང་ … །རྡེལ་ནག་མང་ཤར་བསོད་ནམས་ཆུང་། །ཆོས་ལ་རྒྱབ་བརྟེན་བྱ་བར་བཟང་། །མི་ཡི་བྲན་བྱེད་དགོས་པ་ཡིན། … །བུ་བཞི་དཀར་པོ་བྱུང༌། །ཕ་བས་བུ་བཟང་ … །བུ་བཞི་ནག་བྱུང་བྱིས་པ་ནི། །གསོ་དཀའ་ … །འདྲེས་མ་ཕྱེད་སོས་ཕྱེད་མི་སོས། … །གྲོགས་བཞི་དཀར་ན་ … །དེ་ཡི་རིགས་པས་གཞན་རྣམས་ཤེས།

The mothers white: the forebears flourish, a noble line. The four mothers
black: the forebears decline, little kindness from the father, support to
be sought from others; half white and half black: the forebears' joys and
sorrows, rise and decline great. Many white pebbles among the life force,
body, power and luck: carried by others, works accomplished, all
understood by oneself; many black: little merit, good to rely on the
dharma, one must serve others. The four sons white: a son better than his
father, who holds the forebears' land; black, children hard to raise;
mixed, half live and half do not. The four friends white … "By this
reasoning know the rest."

**The birth, the twenty-one children** (pp. 338–342, img. 348–352): from the
secret *yang tig*, seven good children (of nature, of a karmic line, of
spontaneous merit, of nourished elements, of auspicious bones, of great
glory given by gods and demons, of a noble life), seven middling and seven
bad (from the butcher who brings down demons to the one whose own life
conquers him), the editors numbering them (༡)–(༧) in each group; then each
defined by conditions across the year, month, planet, mansion, half of the
month and hour of birth, the parents' years, the graves and the trigram
and mewa, e.g. the first:

> ལོ་ཡིས་བསྐྱེད་དང་ཕ་ཡིས་གསོ། ། ཟླ་བས་བསྐྱེད་དང་མ་ཡིས་བསྟེན། །གཟའ་བཞིའི་དུར་དུ་མ་སོང་བ། །ངན་ཀླུང་གང་གིས་མ་གོས་པ། །གཟའ་སྐར་བཟང་དང་ཡར་གྱི་ངོ་། །སྲོད་དང་སྔ་དྲོའི་དུས་ལ་བཙས། །དེ་ནི་རང་བཞིན་ཆགས་པས་བཟང་(༡)།

Engendered by the year and nourished by the father, engendered by the
month and supported by the mother, not gone into the grave of the four
planets, stained by no bad luck, a good planet and mansion and the waxing
half, born at dusk or in the morning: that is the good child of nature.
Some conditions are not dates (dreams of fierce gods, p. 339); RD19 sorts
which can be reckoned.

**The birth, the whole** (vol. 1, p. 349, img. 359), at the chapter's close:

> འབྲས་བུ་རེ་གཉིས་མི་རྟོགས་པས། །འབྲས་ཤུགས་ཆེ་ཆུང་མང་ཉུང་རྣམས། །ཡས་མས་ཀུན་དུ་ལེགས་བརྟགས་ཏེ། །བཟང་འབྲས་ཆེ་མང་བཟང་འབྲས་ཕྱིན། །ངན་འབྲས་ཆེ་མང་ངན་པ་སྟེ། །མཉམ་ན་འབྲིང་ཡིན་དེ་ཡང་ནི། །འཕྲུལ་གྱི་བྱ་བྱེད་རིམ་གྲོ་ཡིས། །ངན་འབྲས་བཟང་པོར་འགྱུར་བ་དང་། །འཕྲལ་རྐྱེན་བཟང་འབྲས་ངན་འབྲས་ལ། །འགྲོ་བ་ཡང་ནི་སྲིད་པ་སྟེ།

Not deciding on one or two results, examine well throughout, above and
below, the results' force, great or small, and their number; where the good
results are greater and more, the good comes; where the bad, the bad; even,
middling. And by the rites (རིམ་གྲོ, rim gro) the bad results can turn good,
and by passing conditions the good can go to the bad.

**The year of age, the notes' verdict** (vol. 2, p. 490, img. 498):

> རྡེའུ་བོར་ལུགས་གཞུང་བཞིན། ཁ་དམར་ གསུམ་ལྟ་བུའ ང་གཅིག་ལ་བརྩི། དགྲ་འཕྲད་ནག་གཉིས་ཀྱང་སོ་སོར་མི་བརྩི་ … ་མང་དཀར་པོ། ནག་མང་ནག་པོ། མཉམ་ན་ཁྲ་བོ་ … བཞག

The pebbles laid as the text says; for the verdict even a three counts as
one, and the enemy's two black are not counted apart; more white, white;
more black, black; even, a speckled one is laid. (Open question 167.)

**The marriage, the notes** (vol. 2, pp. 524–526, img. 532–534):

> ལོ་ངན་སྐོར་ཕོ་མོ་གཉིས་འདོམས་ན་ངན་སྟེ་བཏང་གཅོད་ནག་གཅིག་ཡིན་ན་ཁྲ་གཉིས་ཀ་མིན་ན་རྡེའུ་དཀར་པོ། ཁྱད་པར་བུད་མེད་ལོ་ངན་བྱུང་ན་ངན། འདི་རྣམས་ཟུང་རེ་སྡེབ་མི་དགོས་སྤྱིར་རྩི།

The bad years: both the man and the woman meeting them is bad; the
deciding pebble black, one of them a speckled one, neither a white one;
above all a bad year of the woman is bad. These are not joined pair by
pair but reckoned in general.

> དེ་ཡང་སྲོག་ལས་ཀྱི་ཆུང་མ་སོགས་སོ་སོར་སྦྱར་ལ་སྤྱིར་བཟང་ངན་བལྟ། ནང་ཕྲད་ས་ཆུ་ལས་ཀྱི་ཆུང་མ་དང་གཞན་གསུམ་གཉེན་གྱི་ཆུང་མར་གཏོགས་ཞེས་གཡའ་སེལ་དུ་བཤད།

Each aspect joined to its kind, the life force a spouse of karma and so on,
and then good and bad looked at in general; earth and water meeting
themselves a spouse of karma, the other three a spouse of kin, as a work
the notes name *Ya sel* (གཡའ་སེལ, g.ya' sel; not identified) says.

> འབྱུང་བའི་སྤུན་གྲངས་ནི་ཕོ་མོའི་སྲོག་ལུས་ ཀླུང་སྐྱེས་སྨེ་བཅས་ཀྱི་འབྱུང་ཁམས་ བཞིན་བྱ་སྟེ། … གང་མང་བ་དེ་རྒྱལ། མང་ཉུང་མཉམ་ན་ལེགས། དེ་ཡང་སྲོག་སོགས་ལྔ་ཡི་རྡེའུ་གྲངས་གཅིག་ཏུ་བསྡོམ་ནས། ཕོ་མོ་སུ་མང་བ་ལྟ་བ་ལས་སྲོག་ ན ས་བརྟག་རྒྱུ་མིན་པར་བཤད། གཉིས་ཀ་གཅིག་ཏུ་ པ་པས། ཡ་བྱུང་ན་མི་མཐུན་ཆ་བྱུང་ན་མཐུན་པ་ཡིན་པར་བལྟའོ།

The siblings' number by the elements: the man's and the woman's life
force, body, [power,] luck and birth mewa by their elements' counts;
whichever is more wins, even is good; the five counts are summed into one,
and who has more is looked at, not the life force alone; the two together,
odd disagree, even agree. (Open question 166.)

On p. 526 the bride's hidden words are read as the reckoning's faults:
"poor" the power small in the reckoning of karma, "old" … and «ལས་འབྲས་ཏེ་རྩིས་སྤྱིར་ངན་པ», "the result of karma: the reckoning bad in general".

**The marriage, point 13** (vol. 1, p. 364, img. 374): each one's life force
taking breath on the other's year, «དཀར་པོ་མང་མཐུན་ཉུང་མི་མཐུན», many white
agree, few disagree.

**The illness** (vol. 1, p. 435, img. 445):

> དངོས་གཞི་ནད་ཀྱི་ངོས་བཟུང་ནི། །ལོ་ཟླ་ཞག་དུས་སྲོག་ཁམས་བཞི། … །ལོ་ཟླ་ཞག་དུས་བཞི་ཡི་ཁམས། །གང་མང་གཅིག་གི་ཁམས་རྡེལ་ལྔ། … །བཤད་ཚུལ་མང་ཡང་བསྡུ་བ་ན། །འབྱུང་བ་ལྔ་ལ་འདུས་པ་སྟེ། །ཐམས་ཅད་འདུས་ན་འདུས་པའི་ནད །

The illness identified: the year, month, day, hour and life force; of the
four times' elements, whichever is most, the five element pebbles of
that one; told in many ways, gathered they come to the five elements, and
when all gather, a gathered illness (འདུས་པའི་ནད, 'dus pa'i nad). The notes
(vol. 2, p. 497, img. 505): «འབྱུང་ཁམས་གང་མང་ཤས་ཆེ་བའི་ནད་དུ་ངོས་བཟུང་། ཤིང་གཉིས་མེ་ … གསུམ་ལྟ་བུ་བྱུང་ན།», the
element most and greatest in share is taken as the illness; where two
wood and three fire come, the shares are named. *Corrected 2026-10-21
(RD22, on the scan): the elided words read «ཤིང་གཉིས་མེ་གཉིས་ལྕགས་གསུམ», two
wood, two fire, three iron, iron's the illness by its greater share.*

**The thirty starting points** (vol. 1, p. 471, img. 481): «དེ་ཡང་གང་བརྩི་སོ་སོ་ལ། །རིག་པས་དཔྱད་དེ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས། །གཞན་ཡང་ཡར་གྱི་རྡེའུ་དཀར། །མར་རྡེལ་ནག་པར་གྱུར་པ་ན། །ཕ་མེས་བཟང་ཡང་རང་མགོ་ཞན། … །ཚེ་སྟོད་བཟང་ཡང་ཚེ་སྨད་ངན།»: for each
reckoned, lay the verdict examining with intelligence; and where the
upper pebbles are white and the lower black, the forebears good but
oneself weak, the first part of life good and the last bad (and the
reverse).

**The small reckonings of ch. 27** (vol. 2, p. 22, img. 30): «བསྟན་པའི་ཁ་དམར་འབྲས་བུ་བརྗོད། །རགས་པར་སྲོག་ལ་ཚེའི་འགལ་རྐྱེན། །ལུས་ནད་དབང་ནོར་ཀློང་བསོད་ནམས། །སྨེ་བ་ལྷ་འདྲེའི་ཁྲོམ་བྱད་སོགས། །དཀར་ནག་ཁ་དམར་དང་བསྟུན་བརྗོད། །ངན་ན་སྲོག་བསྐྱེད་ཚེ་བསྲིང་ཐབས། །ལུས་ཀྱི་ནད་སྡུག་བཟློག་པ་དང་། །དབང་ཐང་ལྷ་གཡང་འབྱོར་པ་སྤེལ། །ཀླུང་རྟ་བསོད་ནམས་སྟོབས་བསྐྱེད་ཅིང་། །མི་ཁ་དགྲ་བཟློག་སྨེ་བ་ལ། །དེ་བསྟུན་ལྷ་འདྲེ་བཅོ་བ…»: the verdicts'
results told: roughly, the life force the adversities of life, the body
illness, the power wealth, the luck merit, the mewa gods and demons, the
crowd's curse and the like; told as the white and black verdicts fall. If
bad: means to raise the life force and lengthen life, to turn back the
body's illness and pain, to increase the power's god of fortune and
wealth, to strengthen the luck's merit, to turn back gossip and enemies,
and for the mewa to treat the gods and demons. p. 23 (img. 31): where the
signs fall, black, else white, «ཁ་དམར་སྤྱི་ལྟར་གདགས», the verdict laid "as in general".

**The notes on condensing** (vol. 2, p. 513, img. 521): of the planets'
and mansions' reckoning «སྤྱིར་བསྡོམས … ངོས་འཛིན་སྟོང་བརྒྱད་བརྒྱ་ཉེར་བརྒྱད་བགྲང་འདུག་ཀྱང་རྒྱས་སྤྲོས་ཙམ་སྟེ་གནད་ལག་ཏུ་ལེན་པ་ལ་ … བསྡུས་ཏེ་དོན་སྙིང་པོར་དྲིལ་བ་ལེགས་སོ།», the sum is counted at 1,828 identifying
pebbles, but that is elaboration; for putting the point into practice it
is good to condense it to its essence. And vol. 1, p. 258 (img. 268):
«ལོག་མན་དགུ་མིག་སྐོར་འགོ་སོགས། །རང་རང་ཁ་དམར་སྐབས་སུ་སྟོན།», the progressed
sign, the nine-multiples, the starting points and the rest each show
their verdict in their own place.

**The deciding texts** (vol. 1, pp. 245, 248, img. 255, 258): among the
classes of the reckoning's texts «བཏང་གཅོད» (p. 245), and among Khampa Tramo's
(ཁམས་པ་ཁྲ་མོ, khams pa khra mo) commentaries a *Lamp of the Magic of Deciding* (བཏང་གཅོད་འཕྲུལ་གྱི་སྒྲོན་མེ, btang
gcod 'phrul gyi sgron me; p. 248): a genre of texts on the deciding itself.
RD25.

## What R4 could build, once the readings are in

A sketch to be cut to what the readings confirm, not a decision. For each
reckoning, a **Together** part above its sections: the verdict of each
aspect (step 2), the set readings that hold (step 3), whose aspect matters
most and which part of life (step 4), the class the whole falls into and
WB's rule for the whole, good, middling or bad with its reasons named
(step 5), and the remedies gathered from the rows below, each with its
source, one *Remedy* row as Y1 has it. No number is shown that WB does
not count. The sections stay as they are, below it.

Built for the marriage match on 2026-10-22 (ROADMAP R4, block 58): the
kinds of wife read whole, the overruling stated, the remedies gathered;
not for the birth, whose hundred pebbles and twenty-one children wait on
open questions 184–185.

## The readings

RD18–RD28 in the roadmap's *Readings* table, in the order of the ladder:
RD18 the verdict pebble in every chapter; RD19 the birth's sets, its
twenty-one children and its whole; RD20 the year of age and the
household; RD21 the marriage match; RD22 the illness; RD23 the dead, the
thirty, the circles and the moment; RD24 whose aspect, which part of life,
and the reckonings with each other; RD25 ch. 20's classes and the deciding
texts; RD26 Mipham's notes on condensing; RD27 Dharmaśrī's *Moonbeams*
(DZ) on how the abridgement closes a reckoning; RD28 written reckonings, the
practitioners' own form, below WB.

## Open

- Question 167: the tie of a verdict, "none" as built or a speckled one.
- Question 166, narrowed by the notes' p. 525 (above).
- Whether WB ever reads two reckonings together (RD24).
- Which of the twenty-one children's conditions are dates, which are
  observations (RD19).

## The verdict pebble, chapter by chapter (RD18)

Read 2026-10-20 on BDRC's scans of the 1996 print (vol. 1 I1KG12906,
image = page + 10; vol. 2 I1KG12907, image = page + 8): every line that
holds «ཁ་དམར» or «བཏང་གཅོད» in ch. 21–30 and the notes' rules on laying
a verdict, found in the etext and Yigdzin-1, each read on the scan in
half-line crops at 3× (the doubtful words at 6–7×). MITRA was run as
witness over vol. 1, img. 311, 323, 329, 339, 359, 455 and vol. 2,
img. 30, 31, 60–62, 180, 498, 508, 509: no flag in the quoted lines of
p. 490; in the others its flags are its own slips (ཇེའུ for རྡེའུ, བྱེད
for ཕྱེད) or Yigdzin-1's, each settled on the scan (p. 349's «སྨྲོས»,
p. 329's last line and vol. 2, p. 50's l. 28, both dropped by
Yigdzin-1); on the notes' pp. 500–501 and vol. 1, p. 301 it agrees with
Yigdzin-1 on 12–56 % of syllables only, and those lines rest on the eye.
Where a passage was
already read on the scan for another topic file, the file is named and
its reading kept. Gyurme Dorje's charts 6.2 (p. 228) and 8.1 (p. 296)
were read on archive.org's page images.

### What the words name

**The verdict** (ཁ་དམར, kha dmar) is in WB a judgement that is laid,
told and shown: it is laid («གདགས», «བོར», «བཞག»), spoken («སྨྲོས», «བརྗོད»)
and shown («སྟོན», «བསྟན»), and it has a pebble of its own («ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ»,
«ཁ་དམར་རྡེའུ»). No passage of ch. 21–30 or of the notes gives it a
colour, a mark or a material. Where the words mean "red mouth" or "red
face" they describe beings, not pebbles: a demon's mouth gaping in the
illness chapter (vol. 1, p. 441, img. 451, l. 5, «ཟེར་ནས་ཁ་དམར་གདང་ངེ་འདུག»), the harmful spirits of the dead (སྲི, sri) in the row
of the birth's year-horses, the red-mouthed life-demon and loss-sri beside
the flesh-eating field-sri
(vol. 1, p. 305, img. 315, l. 23, «གྲལ་ན་སྲོག་བདུད་ཁ་དམར་འདུག»;
p. 306, img. 316, l. 6, «གྲལ་ན་གོད་སྲི་ཁ་དམར་འདུག»; the parallel
lines have «ཞིང་སྲི་ཤ་ཟན»), a red-faced woman (vol. 2, p. 60, img. 68,
«བུད་མེད་གདོང་ཁ་དམར», etext) and a red-mouthed tube in a rite (vol. 2,
p. 105, img. 113, «ཐེམ་ཁ་དམར», etext). So the
"red mark" by which [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md) renders p. 261's
«རེ་རེ་ལ་ཡང་ཁ་དམར་གདགས» is the word taken apart, not a thing WB
describes: there, as everywhere, it is "on each, lay the verdict". Three
passages show that the verdict is a pronouncement:

- vol. 1, p. 349 (img. 359, l. 26–27), the close of the birth: «ཡས་མས་ཆ་བསྡུར་ཞིབ་བརྟགས་ཏེ། །ཁ་དམར་མ་ནོར་ཚུལ་བཞིན་སྨྲོས།», compare above
  and below, examine closely, and speak the verdict without error, as it
  is (Yigdzin-1's «སྤྲོས» is the scan's «སྨྲོས»);
- vol. 1, p. 448 (img. 458, l. 9–10), a rite for the sick: an effigy is
  laid in the bed, the doctor takes its pulse, «འཆི་འདུག་ཅེས་པའི་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།», lay the verdict "it is dying";
- vol. 2, p. 22 (img. 30, l. 18), the small reckonings: «དཀར་ནག་ཁ་དམར་དང་བསྟུན་བརྗོད།», tell [the results] as the white and black
  verdicts fall.

**The deciding pebble** (བཏང་གཅོད, btang gcod) is the pebble laid by the
count, and WB uses the two words together and in turn: «བཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་རེ» (vol. 1, p. 385), «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེལ»
(vol. 2, p. 46), «བཏང་གཅོད་ངོས་འཛིན་རྡེའུ་བདུན» (vol. 2, p. 47). In two
places it is the plain verb "to decide": the eight starting points that
decide whether a body is burnt or kept (vol. 1, p. 468, img. 478, l. 1,
«ཁ་ནས་ཕུང་པོ་བསྲེག་སྒྲུབ་ཀྱི། །བཏང་གཅོད་རྩིས་འགོ་རྣམ་བརྒྱད་ལ། །དང་པོ་དམར་རོ་འབྲུམ་བུའི་རོ། །མེ་ལ་བསྲེག་གམ་མི་བསྲེག་བརྩི།»), and the
diviner's arrow of decision among the implements (vol. 2, p. 147,
img. 155, l. 13, «མདེའུ་བཏང་གཅོད་ཐད་དྲང་ལ།», etext and Yigdzin-1, not
read on the scan). Dharmaśrī's *Moonbeams* writes it «སྟངས་གཅོད» (KD
vol. 3, etext pp. 522, 529, 533, 535; etext only).

### What is counted: the places, not the pebbles

WB's rule for the year of age (vol. 1, p. 381, img. 391, l. 2–4, read
again on the scan; the whole passage in [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)):

> །དེ་ལ་ཁ་སྦྲང་རྡེལ་འགྲེམ་ལུགས། །མི་མཐུན་མང་ཡང་དཀར་ནག་གི། །གསུམ་གཉིས་ལ་སོགས་མི་འདྲེན་པར། །ངོ་ཐེག་དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པ་ཡི། །གང་མང་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་བོར་བ།

The ways of laying out the pebbles (the print's «ཁ་སྦྲང», not
identified) differ; [here,] not taking in the threes, twos and so on of
white and black, count the white and black of the faces (ངོ་ཐེག, ngo
theg), and lay the verdict pebble on whichever are more.

Mipham's notes say what a "face" is (vol. 2, p. 490, img. 498, l. 5–8,
read on the scan at 2.2× and 6×, MITRA agreeing):

> །རྡེའུ་བོར་ལུགས་གཞུང་བཞིན། ཁ་དམར་ནི་མ་འཕྲད་དཀར་གསུམ་ལྟ་བུའང་གཅིག་ལ་བརྩི། དགྲ་འཕྲད་ནག་གཉིས་ཀྱང་སོ་སོར་མི་བརྩི་བར་གནས་དེའི་དཀར་ནག་བསྒྲངས་ལ་དཀར་མང་དཀར་པོ། ནག་མང་ནག་པོ། མཉམ་ན་ཁྲ་བོ་བཞག

The pebbles are laid as the text says. For the verdict, even the three
white of a meeting with the mother count as one, and the two black of a
meeting with the enemy are not counted one by one either: count the
white and black of each place (the print's «བསྒྲངས», for བགྲངས, read at
6×); more white, a white one; more black, a black one; even, lay a
speckled one (ཁྲ་བོ, khra bo).

So each of the six basic signs that meets an aspect is one place, and
counts once by its colour, whatever the number of its pebbles. The notes
say it again for the reckoning for the dead (p. 500, img. 508, l. 14–15,
on the scan): «བཟང་དྲུག་དཀར་ངན་དྲུག་ནག་མ་འཕྲད་དཀར་གསུམ་སོགས་སྤྱི་བཞིན་བོར། ཁ་དམར་ཀྱང་སྤྱི་ལྟར་རོ།», the good six [stages] white, the bad
six black, the mother's meeting three white and so on, laid as in
general; the verdict too as in general. What a place with both colours
counts as (a son's meeting, one white and one black; the breath-taking
and the womb among the stages) neither WB nor the notes say here; in the
reckoning for the dead the notes put a mixed year with the speckled
(p. 502, img. 510, l. 9, on the scan: «གྲོགས་ལོ་ཆེ་དཀར་ཆུང་ནག་ལྟ་བུའང་ཁྲ་བོར་གཏོགས།», a friend's year, white for the elder and black
for the younger, also belongs to the speckled).

### What a tie lays

Every rule for a tie that WB and its notes give lays a third value, never
nothing:

| Where | The rule, on the scan | A tie |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Notes, p. 490 (img. 498, l. 7–8), the year of age | «དཀར་མང་དཀར་པོ། ནག་མང་ནག་པོ། མཉམ་ན་ཁྲ་བོ་བཞག» | a speckled pebble |
| Ch. 29, vol. 2, p. 46 (img. 54, l. 4–5), the reckoning for the dead, its deciding pebbles | «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེལ། །དཀར་མང་དཀར་ལ་ནག་མང་ནག །ཕྱེད་མ་ཁྲ་བོར་བོར་བ་ཡི། །འཐབ་རྡེལ་ལྔ་བཅུ་རྩ་ལྔ་འབྱུང་།» | half and half, a speckled one (until now read on the Gser thang reprint only) |
| Ch. 29, p. 46 (img. 54, l. 16–18), the share of the rites, its root pebbles | «ཁམས་རྡེལ་གཉིས་དཀར་དཀར་པོ་དང་། །གཉིས་ཀ་ནག་ན་ནག་པོར་བོར། །གཅིག་དཀར་གཅིག་ནག་ཁྲ་བོར་བོར།» | one white and one black, a speckled one |
| Ch. 29, p. 47 (img. 55, l. 3), the same | «དེ་ལྟར་དཀར་མང་དཀར་པོ་དང་། །ནག་མང་ནག་པོ་བཏང་སྙོམས་ཁྲའི། །ཁ་དམར་དང་བཅས་རྡེལ་བཅུ་གཅིག» | even (བཏང་སྙོམས, btang snyoms), a speckled verdict |
| Ch. 29, p. 47 (img. 55, l. 8–9), the rebirth | «རྡེའུ་ཐམས་ཅད་གོ་བསྟུན་ལ། །དཀར་ཤས་ཆེ་ན་དཀར་པོ་གཅིག །གཅིག་དཀར་གཅིག་ནག་དཀར་ནག་གཉིས། །ནག་ན་ནག་པོའི་རྡེའུ་བོར།» | one white and one black: both, a white and a black |
| Ch. 29, p. 50 (img. 58, l. 27–28), the thirteenth point | «དཀར་མང་དཀར་པོ་ནག་པོ་མང་། །ནག་རྡེལ་སྙོམས་ན་དཀར་ནག་ཟུང་།» (l. 28, which Yigdzin-1 drops, read on the scan) | even, a pair of white and black |
| Notes, p. 500 (img. 508, l. 17–19), the share of the rites | «བཏང་གཅོད་གཉིས་ཀ་དཀར་ན་དཀར་པོ་བོར། གཉིས་ཀ་ནག་ན་ནག་པོ། གཅིག་དཀར་གཅིག་ནག་ན་ཁྲ་བོ་བོར་ཏེ། དཀར་པོ་ལ་འདུར་ཐང་ཆེ་བ་ཐོབ།» | one of each, a speckled one; the white wins a large share |
| *Moonbeams*, KD vol. 3, etext p. 534; DZ f. 28b (img. 060), the reckoning for the dead (O18, on the scan) | «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གྱི། །རྡེའུ་བཅུ་གཉིས་ལོགས་སུ་བོར། །དཀར་ལ་དཀར་པོ་ལྡོག་པ་ནག །མཉམ་ན་ཕྱེད་མ» (KD's «ཕྱེད་མི» is the scan's «ཕྱེད་མ»; «ཁྱད་པར་དུ» opens the next sentence) | even, a half (ཕྱེད་མ, phyed ma) |

And the speckled or half verdict is read, not left blank:

- the rebirth (vol. 2, p. 70, img. 78, l. 13–14, on the scan): «སྐྱེ་གནས་བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་དཀར། །གཤིན་པོ་ལྷར་སྐྱེ་ནག་པ་ན། །ངན་སོང་ཁྲ་བོ་མི་རུ་སྐྱེ།», the deciding pebble of the rebirth white, the dead is
  born a god; black, in the lower realms; speckled, a human;
- the household of the dead (vol. 2, p. 54, img. 62, l. 5–18, on the
  scan): before the great harm, «རྡེའུ་ནི། །དཀར་ནག་ཕྱེད་མར་བྱུང་བ་ན། །ཕོ་བརྒྱུད་བྱེས་སུ་འཁྱར་བ་ཡོད།», the pebbles half white and half black,
  the male line wanders abroad; and a reading each for half and half
  before the small harm, the mother's, son's, power's, friend's and
  enemy's year («ཆད་ཆུང་མདུན་དུ་ཕྱེད་མར་བྱུང་།», «མ་ལོའི་མདུན་དུ་ཕྱེད་མར་བྱུང་།», …, «དགྲ་ལོའི་མདུན་དུ་ཕྱེད་མར་བྱུང་།»);
- the birth (vol. 1, p. 345, img. 355, l. 28, to p. 346, img. 356,
  l. 1, on the scan), the girl's luck: «བུ་མོའི་ཀླུང་ལ་ལྟེ་བསྒྱུར་ཏེ། །དེ་ཡང་དཀར་ནག་ཁྲ་གསུམ་སོགས། །ལོགས་སུ་ཁ་དམར་བསྟན་ལས་ཤེས།», turn the
  centre for the girl's luck; white, black and speckled, the three, and
  the rest: know them from the verdicts shown apart;
- the rebirth again (vol. 2, p. 71, img. 79, l. 7–8, on the scan):
  «ཀླུང་རྟས་སྐྱེ་སྲིད་རབ་འབྲིང་ནི། །མ་དང་རང་རབ་གྲོགས་བུ་འབྲིང་། །དགྲ་ཉིད་ཐ་མར་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།», by the luck, the rebirth best or middling:
  the mother and itself best, the friend and son middling, the enemy last;
  lay the verdict so. A verdict of three grades, the middle one the mixed
  relations.

**Open question 167, settled.** The year of age's tie lays a speckled
predictive pebble (the notes, p. 490), as every other tie rule of WB and
its notes lays a third value; and each meeting counts once by its colour,
the mother's three white as one white, the enemy's two black as one
black (the same lines, and p. 381's "not the threes and twos"). WB's
p. 381 states neither in so many words; the notes, Mipham's, are its
reading of the line.

**Gyurme Dorje's chart 6.2** (p. 228, a man of a fire dragon year in an
earth tiger year), its six meetings and its predictive column, read on
the page image:

| Aspect | Year | Progressed sign | Trigram | Mewa (སྨེ་བ, sme ba) | Stage | Hour | Predictive |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vitality | ×× | ○○ | ×× | ○○○ | × | ○○○ | ○× |
| Body | ○ | ○ | ×× | ○○○ | × | ○○ | ○ |
| Destiny | ○× | ○○ | ○○○ | × | ○ | ×× | ○× |
| Luck | ×× | × | × | ○× | ○○○ | ○○○ | × |

Counted pebble by pebble, all four aspects have more white (8:5, 7:3,
7:4, 7:5). Counted by places, as the notes count, the
vitality is three to three, a tie, and Dorje lays ○×; the body four to
two, ○; the luck three or two white to four black, ×. The destiny comes
to ○× only if its son's meeting (○×) counts as black: three to three.
His column is the notes' rule with the speckled tie, and a son's meeting
counted black; the second part no text read here states. His text
(p. 228) says the four are laid "alongside whichever pile is the larger",
and quotes the *Moonbeams* f. 28b; that passage (KD vol. 3, etext
p. 527: «དེ་ནས་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་བཞི་ཡང་། །དཀར་ནག་མང་ཉུང་བགྲངས་བྱས་ལ། །དཀར་གསུམ་དཀར་གཉིས་…སོགས་སུ་བོར།», etext only) is RD27's. Chart 8.1
(p. 296, the health divination) lays its nineteen pebbles and no verdict.

### The verdict in each reckoning

Each line read on the scan unless marked; the passages already in other
topic files are named there.

**Ch. 21** (vol. 1, p. 258, img. 268, l. 16; in
[year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)): «ལོག་མན་དགུ་མིག་སྐོར་འགོ་སོགས། །རང་རང་ཁ་དམར་སྐབས་སུ་སྟོན།», the progressed sign, the nine-multiples,
the starting circles and the rest each show their own verdict in their
place: no verdict over them all.

**Ch. 22, the birth.**

- p. 260–261 (img. 270–271; in [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)):
  136 pebbles, of them twenty deciding ones, laid by the count: «བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཉི་ཤུ་ལ། །སྲོག་གི་རྡེའུ་དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས། …། །དེ་ལ་ཤན་འབྱེད་རྡེའུ་བཞག །རེ་རེ་ལ་ཡང་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།»: for the twenty, count the white
  and black of the life force, the body, the power and the luck; lay the
  pebble that tells them apart, and on each a verdict.
- p. 262 (img. 272): «རྡེལ་བབས་བལྟས་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།», look at how the
  pebbles fall and lay the verdict; «དེ་ཡང་ཁ་དམར་རིམ་པ་དང་། །རྡེལ་ཕྲན་ངོ་ཐོག་བཅས་པ་རྣམས། །རིམ་པར་བཀོད།», the verdicts in their order,
  with the small pebbles, set out in order; then «གཅེས་རྡེལ་བཞི་ཡི་ཁ་དམར་ལ།», the verdicts of the four precious pebbles, each white or
  black with its reading.
- p. 279 (img. 289, l. 10) «ངན་པའི་ཁ་དམར་འདི་ལྟ་སྟེ།», the bad verdict is
  this; p. 281 (img. 291, l. 17) «གཞན་ཡང་གཅོད་འདྲལ་ཁ་དམར་བསྟན།», the
  verdicts of the aspects cutting one another; p. 283 (img. 293, l. 11)
  «དེ་ནི་བསྡུས་པའི་ཁ་དམར་ཡིན། །རྒྱས་པ་ལྟར་ན་འདི་བཞིན་ཏེ།», that is the
  short verdict; the long one is this; p. 294 (img. 304, l. 14) «ལུས་ཀྱི་རྒྱལ་འཕམ་ལྷག་ཆད་དང་། །སོ་ཐུབ་ཁ་དམར་སྟོན་པ་སྟེ།», the verdicts of the
  body winning or losing, surplus or lack: each section's reading is its

- p. 301 (img. 311, l. 23): «དཔེར་ན་ལི་འདོམ་མང་བ་ན། །ཡུགས་ཀྱིས་ལན་པར་ཁ་དམར་མདགས། །དེ་ཡི་རིགས་པ་ཀུན་ལ་སྦྱར།», where the trigram li
  (ལི, li) meets the
  most [of the year, month, day and hour], lay the verdict that widowhood
  answers it (the print's «མདགས» for གདགས); apply this reasoning to all:
  the trigram that meets most decides.
- p. 313 (img. 323, l. 23–24) and p. 319 (img. 329, l. 9–10): the

  the middle planet is this; «ཕྱི་གཟའ་ནང་གཟའ་གཉིས་ཀྱིས་ནི། །བར་གཟའ་དག་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།», by the outer and inner planets lay the verdict on the

  verdict that the newborn will not live.
- p. 329 (img. 339, last line): «བརྒྱ་ཕྲག་གཅིག་གི་ཁ་དམར་ནི། ༄སྲོག་གི་རང་རྡེལ་དཀར་བ་ན། །རིན་ཆེན་ཚེ་ཡི་གཏེར་ཞེས་བྱ།», the verdicts of the
  hundred: where the life force's own pebble is white, "a precious

- p. 345–346: the three values, above. p. 349 (img. 359, l. 22–27):

  and number of its results, even middling (the etext's reading of the
  passage, above, stands; RD19 reads it whole).

**Ch. 23, the marriage.** p. 356 (img. 365, l. 28, to img. 366, l. 1):
«རྡེལ་བོར་ཀེག་རྩིས་ཇི་བཞིན་ནོ། །དེ་ཡི་ཁ་དམར་འདི་བཞིན་གདགས།», the

(img. 369, l. 4, «ཁ་དམར་ཁུག་པ་དག་དང་མཚུངས།», the verdict as for the
drawing-in (ཁུག་པ, khug pa) of the lines before it, the mewa that draw
one another; l. 13, «རྡེའུ་ཟུང་མཐུན་ཡ་མི་མཐུན། །ཕོ་མོ་རྡེའུ་གང་མང་རྒྱལ།», pebbles in pairs agree, odd disagree; of man and woman,
whoever has more pebbles wins; RD21's).

**Ch. 24, the year of age** (all read on the scan for O10d and RD14, in
[year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)): p. 380, four verdict pebbles among
the forty-seven («གཅོད་འདྲལ་ཉེར་བཞི་ཁ་དམར་བཞི»); p. 381, the rule
above; pp. 384–385, the readings of each verdict white or black and of
all four alike, then «དེ་ནི་དཀྱུས་མའི་ཁ་དམར་ཡིན།།», that is the plain
verdict, «གཞན་ཡང་ཁ་དམར་ཅི་རིགས་གདགས། …། །ཁྱད་པར་སྡེ་བཞིའི་ཁ་དམར་བསྡུས།», lay further verdicts as fits; above all, gather the four classes'
verdicts (RD20's); p. 385, each member of the household «བཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་རེ», one verdict pebble for each aspect; p. 386, the
verdict of the household's left-over pebble, white two, white and black,
black one, black two, each read; p. 396, «སྤར་ཁ་སོ་སོའི་ཁ་དམར་རོ།།»; p. 401, «སྨེ་རེའི་ཁ་དམར»; p. 404, «སྨེ་རོའི་བབས་ཚད་ཁ་དམར་ཡིན།»;
p. 407, «ད་ནི་སྤྱི་ཡི་ཁ་དམར་ལ། །ཁྱིམ་ནང་གཅིག་དཀར་མང་ཚོགས་ན།», the
general verdict: where the 1 white gathers most in the household. Each
sign has its own verdict; the four aspects' are the ones counted.

**Ch. 25, the illness.**

- p. 417 (img. 427, l. 8): «བདེན་བརྗོད་རུང་རུང་མ་བརྩིས་ན། །དུས་ཚོད་ལྷ་ཡིས་བློ་ཕྲོགས་ནས། །རྩིས་ཀྱི་ཁ་དམར་ལོག་འགྲོ་བས།», reckoned without the
  invocation of truth, the gods of the hour steal the mind, and the
  reckoning's verdict goes wrong.
- p. 445 (img. 455, l. 18–21), life or death: «ཐ་མར་ཁ་དམར་གདགས་པ་ཡང་། །སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཐང་ཀླུང་རྟ་བཞི། །གུད་དང་ན་ཤི་དུར་ཞུགས་དང་། །འབྱུང་བའི་དགྲ་ཆེན་ལངས་པ་དང་། །བཟའ་ཚང་ཀེག་འདོམ་མང་ན་འཆི། །ལོ་དང་ཟླ་བ་ཞག་དང་དུས། །ཀེག་མེད་དུར་འདོམ་མ་བྱུང་ཞིང་། །འབྱུང་བ་མ་དང་ཕྲད་པ་དང་། །བདུད་གཅོད་ཞི་ནས་ཉེན་མ་ལངས། །ཇི་ལྟར་སྣང་ཡང་མི་འཆི་སྟེ། །ལོ་ཀེག་ཁྲ་མོས་གནོད་མི་ནུས། །གཏོ་དང་ཆོ་གས་ཐུབ་པ་ཡིན།»: lastly, the
  verdict: the four aspects in decline, illness, death and the tomb, the
  elements' great enemy risen, and many obstacles of the household meeting
  there, death; the year, month, day and hour without obstacle or tomb,
  the element meeting its mother, the demon-cutting calmed and no danger
  risen, then whatever it looks like, no death: a speckled year obstacle
  cannot harm, and ransom rites and rituals overcome it.
- p. 445 (img. 455, l. 24–27), the nineteen pebbles for the sick: «ལོ་ཟླ་ཞག་དང་དུས་ཚོད་ལ། །སྲོག་ལུས་གཉིས་ཀྱི་རྡེའུ་ནི། །གཅོད་འདྲལ་རེ་དང་དར་གུད་རེ། །དེ་ལྟར་རྡེའུ་བཅུ་དྲུག་དང་། །ཚེས་གྲངས་གཟའ་སྐར་རེ་རེ་སྟེ། །སྤྱི་བསྡུས་རྡེའུ་བཅུ་དགུ་བོར། །དཀར་ནག་གྲངས་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།»,
  sixteen pebbles of the life force and body against the year, month, day
  and hour, one each for the date, weekday and mansion: by the number of
  white and black, lay the verdict (Dorje's chart 8.1).
- p. 448 (img. 458, l. 9–10), the rite of the verdict "dying", above;
  p. 449 (img. 459, l. 6) «གསང་སྐོར་ཁ་དམར་དག་ཏུ་བསྡུས།», the secret
  circle gathered into the verdicts; p. 450 (img. 460, l. 17) «ཐ་མར་གསོ་ཞིང་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།», last, heal and lay the verdict; p. 452 (img. 462,
  l. 3) «ཧ་ལམ་གྱི་ཁ་དམར་མཁན», the verdict-makers by guesswork.

**Ch. 26, the thirty starting points.** p. 468, the deciding of burning,
above; p. 471 (img. 481, l. 19–21): «དེ་ཡང་གང་བརྩི་སོ་སོ་ལ། །རིག་པས་དཔྱད་དེ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས། །གཞན་ཡང་ཡར་གྱི་རྡེའུ་དཀར། །མར་རྡེལ་ནག་པར་གྱུར་པ་ན། །ཕ་མེས་བཟང་ཡང་རང་མགོ་ཞན།», for each thing reckoned, examine with
intelligence and lay the verdict; where the upper pebbles are white and
the lower black, the forebears good but oneself weak (RD23's, RD24's).

**Ch. 27, the small reckonings** (vol. 2, p. 22, img. 30, l. 8–18; p. 23,
img. 31, l. 5, 12): five verdicts of twenty-five pebbles for each sign,
«དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།», count white and black and lay the
verdict; «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་ཁ་དམར་རེ།», one verdict of the counted
white and black for each of the five; for the signs, «དེ་དག་བབས་ན་རྡེལ་ནག་དང་། །མ་བབ་རྡེའུ་དཀར་པོར་བོར། །ཁ་དམར་སྤྱི་ལྟར་[གདགས་པར་བྱ།]», where
they fall a black pebble, where not a white, the verdict laid as in
general (the bracket, the etext's; the crop of l. 13 does not show it).
RD23 reads the aspects' domains on these pages.

**Ch. 29, the reckoning for the dead.**

- p. 44 (img. 52, l. 23; in [reckonings.md](reckonings.md)): «བཟའ་ཚང་རེ་ལ་ཁ་དམར་རེ།», a verdict for each household; p. 45 (img. 53, l. 14,

- pp. 46–47, 50: the count and the tie, above. p. 48 (img. 56, l. 21–22):

  repetition pebbles with the verdict of the examined white and black.
- p. 50 (img. 58, l. 23–28), the thirteenth point: «རྩ་བའི་རྡེའུ་བཅུ་གཅིག་ནི། །དེ་ནི་གོང་གི་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་། །ཀླུང་རྟ་ཁམས་ཀྱི་ཁ་དམར་རྣམས། །དཀར་ན་དཀར་པོ་ནག་ན་ནག …། །དཀར་མང་དཀར་པོ་ནག་པོ་མང་། །ནག་རྡེལ་སྙོམས་ན་དཀར་ནག་ཟུང་། །གཏོ་རིམ་ཆེ་ཆུང་འབྲིང་ཇི་ལྟར། །དགོས་པའི་ཁ་སྨྲང་འདི་ཡིས་ཤོད།»: the root pebbles are the verdicts of
  the aspects and element above, white where white, black where black, …;
  more white a white, black more a black, even a pair; how great, small or

  the la, the life-spirit (བླ, bla), have verdicts of three kinds, general, special and particular; «གཉིས་པ་བྱེ་བྲག་ཁ་དམར་ནི། །མ་ལོའི་རྡེའུ་རགས་གྱུར་ཀྱང་། །དབང་ལོའི་མདུན་དུ་རྡེལ་ནག་བཏུད།», the particular: even where the mother year's pebbles are
  heavy, black ones laid before the power's year …; «གསུམ་པ་ཁྱད་པར་ཁ་དམར་ནི། །ཆད་ཆེན་མདུན་དུ་རྡེལ་ནག་པ། །བཏུད་མར་བྱུང་ན།», the special:

  the black is reversed (Yigdzin-1's «ལཽ» is the scan's «ལོ»).
- p. 55–56 (img. 63, l. 26, 28; img. 64, l. 5–15; etext and Yigdzin-1,

- p. 70–71: the rebirth, above. p. 74 (img. 82, l. 11–13): «ཁ་དམར་གང་དཀར་ཕྱོགས་དེར་འདུར། །དེ་ཡང་དཀར་པོ་མང་བ་དེར། །ལྷ་ཆེན་ཉི་ཤུ་རྩ་ལྔ་ཡིན། … །ནག་པོ་གང་དུ་མང་བ་དེར། །བདུད་པོ་ཉི་ཤུ་རྩ་ལྔ་ཡིན།», the verdict
  gathers to the white side; where the white are more, the twenty-five
  great gods; where the black, the twenty-five demons. p. 94 (img. 102,
  l. 25): «དེ་གཉིས་ངན་པས་ཁ་དམར་ཐོགས།», both bad, the verdict holds.
- p. 142 (img. 150, l. 24–25), WB's own warning, at the chapter's close:
  «གཞན་མ་རྣམས་ཀྱིས་གཤིན་རྗེལ་རྣམས་ཚུལ་བཞིན་མི་རྟོགས་པའི་བྲེ་གང་རྡེལ་དྲུག་དང་ཉི་ཤུ་མ་ཞེ་བདུན་མ་སོགས་ཀེག་རྡེལ་གཅིག་པུར་གཤིན་རྗེའི་ཁ་དམར་བཏགས།», others, not understanding the pebbles of the dead as they
  are, lay the Lord of Death's verdict on a single obstacle pebble, the
  "six of a measure", the twenty-ones, the forty-sevens and the like.

**Ch. 30, the moment.** p. 163 (img. 171, etext and Yigdzin-1, l. 4–17):
the ground examined, «ཁ་དམར་མ་ནོར་སོ་སོར་གདགས།», each verdict laid
without error; p. 166 (img. 174, l. 10): «རེ་རེ་ལའང་གཅོད་འདྲལ་དང་། །དར་གུད་ཁ་དམར་གསུམ་གསུམ་ཡོད།», for each element three: the cutting, the
rise and decline and the verdict; p. 168 (img. 176, l. 17): «དེ་གཉིས་བསྟུན་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།», lay the verdict by the two together; p. 172
(img. 180; in [reckonings.md](reckonings.md)): «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས།».

**The notes**, besides pp. 490, 500 and 502: p. 501 (img. 509, l. 4–5),
for the dead, «དེ་ཐམས་ཅད་དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པས་ཁ་དམར་རྡེའུ་བཞག བཏང་གཅོད་དངོས་འཛིན་རྡེའུ་བདུན་བགྲང་ལུགས།», all of them counted white and black,
the verdict pebble laid; the way of counting the seven deciding and
identifying pebbles; p. 503 (img. 511, l. 5–6), «ཉི་ཤུ་པོ་དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་སྤྱིའི་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་གཅིག་སྟེ་བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཉེར་གཅིག», one general
verdict pebble of the twenty counted white and black, twenty-one deciding
pebbles: the one place where a verdict is laid over other verdicts by the
same count.

### The rules, in short

- A verdict is laid for each aspect or each examined thing, by counting
  the white and black of its places, each place once; more white, white;
  more black, black (vol. 1, p. 381; vol. 2, pp. 22, 46, 50, 74, 172; vol.
  1, p. 445; the notes, pp. 490, 500, 501).
- A tie lays a speckled pebble, or a white and a black together; never
  nothing (above). A mixed or speckled verdict has readings of its own:
  a human rebirth, the middling grade, the half-and-half readings.
- Each sign has its own verdict in its place (p. 258); they are gathered
  by the same count only where WB says so: the four classes (p. 385,
  RD20), the thirteenth point (vol. 2, p. 50), the notes' one general
  verdict of twenty (p. 503). A verdict on one pebble alone is the
  mistake WB names (vol. 2, p. 142).
- The verdict is spoken and then answered by rites: «ཐ་མར་གསོ་ཞིང་ཁ་དམར་གདགས» (p. 450), the size of the rites by the verdicts (vol. 2,
  p. 50), the bad turned by them (p. 349; p. 445's "rituals overcome it").

- Open question 172 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): what a speckled predictive pebble foretells.
- Open question 173 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): p. 381's «ཁ་སྦྲང» and «ངོ་ཐེག».
- Open question 174 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): vol. 2, p. 50's «ཁ་སྨྲང».

## The year of age gathered: the four classes and the household (RD20)

Read 2026-10-20 on the scan: WB vol. 1, p. 385 (img. 395), l. 15–18, on
the 1996 scan; the notes' section 2 (vol. 2, pp. 489–495, img. 497–503)
on the Gser thang reprint (I3CN5029 img. 495–501), with Yigdzin-1, MITRA
and the 1996 scan beside it. The passages, whole, with their English, are
in [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md), section *The year of age
gathered: the four classes and the household (RD20)*; what the year of
age gives for each step of the ladder above is here.

**Step 2, the verdict of an aspect.** Mipham's (མི་ཕམ, mi pham) notes' line found in the etext
(*Found 2026-10-20*, "the year of age, the notes' verdict") reads on the
scan (vol. 2, p. 490, img. 498, l. 6–8):

> རྡེའུ་བོར་ལུགས་གཞུང་བཞིན། ཁ་དམར་ནི་མ་འཕྲད་དཀར་གསུམ་ལྟ་བུའང་གཅིག་ལ་བརྩི། དགྲ་འཕྲད་ནག་གཉིས་ཀྱང་སོ་སོར་མི་བརྩི་བར་གནས་དེའི་དཀར་ནག་བསྒྲངས་ལ་དཀར་མང་དཀར་པོ། ནག་མང་ནག་པོ། མཉམ་ན་ཁྲ་བོ་བཞག

The pebbles laid as the root text says; for the verdict (ཁ་དམར, kha dmar),
even a mother meeting's three white count as one, and an enemy meeting's
two black are not counted separately: the white and black of each place
counted, more white a white, more black a black, even a speckled one
(ཁྲ་བོ, khra bo) laid. The etext drops «ནི་མ་འཕྲད་དཀར»: it is the mother
meeting's three that count as one. The places are the six against which
each aspect is fought (p. 490, l. 1–5): the current year, the progressed
sign, the trigram of the year, the mewa of the year, the sectors and the
hour of reckoning; the la (བླ, bla) is laid the same way from the natal
mewa, six pebbles and a verdict of its own. The notes' worked example
(p. 489, img. 497, a table) shows it in five rows, vitality, body, power,
luck and la, six places each and a last column, the deciding pebble
(བཏང་གཅོད, btang gcod): the four aspects white, the la black. No row ties,
and each row comes out the same whether a place counts once or every
pebble counts: the example does not settle open question 167 (RD18's).

**Step 3, the set, and the gathering.** WB closes the general verdicts
(vol. 1, p. 385, img. 395, l. 15–17, on the scan):

> གཞན་ཡང་ཁ་དམར་ཅི་རིགས་གདགས། །སྨེ་སྤར་ལ་སོགས་ཀེག་མང་ཡང་། །མདོར་བསྡུས་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཐང་དང་། །ཀླུང་རྟ་བཞི་ཡིན་དེ་བས་ན། །ཁྱད་པར་སྡེ་བཞིའི་ཁ་དམར་བསྡུས།

Lay other verdicts as each case fits; though the obstacles (ཀེག, keg) of
the mewa, the trigram and the rest are many, in brief they come to four,
vitality, body, power and luck; therefore, above all, the verdicts of the
four classes (སྡེ་བཞི, sde bzhi) are gathered. So the year of age's
conclusion is the four aspects' verdicts, read as a set (all four white,
all four black, pp. 384–385), and every other obstacle is read
through them. The notes do that obstacle by obstacle (vol. 2, pp.
492–495, on the scan): a harsh year (རྩུབ་པ, rtsub pa) is harsh or not "by
fighting that year against one's own year's vitality and the rest"
(«རྩུབ་རིགས་འདི་ལ་ལོ་དེ་དང་རང་ལོའི་སྲོག་སོགས་འཐབས་པས་བྱེ་བྲག་ཏུ་རྩུབ་མི་རྩུབ་ཤེས་པ་ཀུན་ལ་གལ་ཆེ», p. 492); a tomb year
(དུར་ལོ, dur lo) met by the vitality and the rest is the own tomb meeting
itself, the slayer's tomb meeting the enemy, "a great danger", and "a small
one" as mother, friend or son (p. 493); the four undertakers (དུར་མི་བཞི,
dur mi bzhi) bad "when they meet the one reckoned's vitality and the rest
as enemy" (p. 494); the *dza zor* (ཛ་ཟོར, dza zor) "if it does not cut the
vitality, there seems to be no fault" («སྲོག་མི་བཅད་ན་ཉེས་པ་མེད་འདྲའོ», p. 494); the
nine-multiples' tomb years «རང་དགྲ་ངན་གཞན་འཇམ», itself or the enemy bad, the
others mild (p. 494); the vitality first throughout.

**Step 4, whose aspect.** WB, pp. 415–416, quoted in year-of-life.md (*The tomb sign*). The same lines
follow WB's sentence on the household's general pebbles directly
(p. 415, img. 425, l. 25): «གཞན་ཡང་བཟའ་ཚང་སྤྱི་རྡེལ་ནི། །དཀར་ནག་གཉིས་ལས་ནག་པོ་མང་། །དེ་ཡི་རིམ་གྲོ་ཤིན་ཏུ་གཅེས།», the
household's general pebbles: of white and black, black the more; their
rites are most precious (which pebbles, and whether the black's majority
is a condition or a statement, is a question below).

**The household.** WB's round for a household (རྒྱ་ནག་རྡེལ་སྐོར, rgya nag rdel
skor; pp. 385–387) takes each member's four gathered verdicts and a
pebble of the mewa of the year, lays all members in one row, folds it
and reads each place by the colour that falls there; the notes (pp.
490–491, on the scan, with a layout of five members and its fold, read
cell by cell) reject reckoners who skip the twenty-four and lay only the
four meetings with the current year («གཞུང་འདིར་དེ་ལྟར་མི་མཛད་དོ», "this text does not
do so"). The household's own verdicts are then: the leftover of the
house's place (two white good fortune, white and black mixed, one black
illness, two black a death, p. 386); many members on one trigram (pp.
396–400) or one mewa colour («སྤྱི་ཡི་ཁ་དམར», p. 407); the general pebbles
(p. 415); with the rites for the household's trigrams and for each
member's four aspects (p. 386). No single score for the house beyond
these.

**In sum.** For one person the year of age gathers in WB's own order:
the four aspects' verdicts; the set they make; the obstacles of the year
read through the aspect each one meets and cuts, the vitality first, an
obstacle that cuts no aspect not counted against the year (the notes,
pp. 492–495); whose aspect matters; and the aspects' remedies. For a
household the round needs every member's year of birth, gender and order
of birth ([year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md), RD14); its verdicts are by
place and colour, not a sum.

### Open

The questions this reading raises (the household's general pebbles, "other
verdicts as each case fits", a son meeting under the notes' rule) are under
open questions 168–170 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)),
from year-of-life.md's RD20 section.

### Searched and not found

- See year-of-life.md, *The year of age gathered* (RD20), *Searched and
  not found*: «སྡེ་བཞིའི་ཁ་དམར» only on vol. 1, p. 385; «བཟའ་ཚང་སྤྱི་རྡེལ» only on
  p. 415; no single household verdict in ch. 24 or the notes' section 2.


## The birth read whole: its sets, its twenty-one children, its close (RD19)

WB vol. 1, ch. 22, pp. 328–342 (img. 338–352) and p. 349 (img. 359); the
notes, vol. 2, pp. 483–485 and 489 (img. 491–493, 497). Read 2026-10-20 on
the Gser thang reprint of the same typesetting (I3CN5028, image = page −
5; I3CN5029, image = page + 6) with Yigdzin-1, BDRC's 1996 scan with
Yigdzin-1 and MITRA as witnesses, every flag and the passages below read
on the scan. The full Tibetan, the hundred's readings, the sets, the
twenty-one children with their conditions sorted, and the notes are in
[year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md), section of the same name; here, what
they give for combining.

**The etext passages above, on the scan.** P. 338 (img. 348) reads as the
etext with its gaps filled: «…དཀར་ནག་ཕྱེད། །བྱུང་བ་ཕ་མེས་སྐྱིད་སྡུག་དང་། །དར་གུད་ཆེ་ཞིང་གཞན་ཡང་ནི། །སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་བཞི་པོ་ཡི། །རྡེལ་དཀར་མང་བྱུང་མིས་ཁུར་འོང་། །བྱ་བ་གྲུབ་ཅིང་རག་མི་ལ། །མ་ལུས་རང་གིས་རྟོགས་པ་ཡིན། །རྡེལ་ནག་མང་ཤར་བསོད་ནམས་ཆུང་། །ཆོས་ལ་རྒྱབ་བརྟེན་བྱ་བར་བཟང་། །མི་ཡི་བྲན་བྱེད་དགོས་པ་ཡིན། །དེ་ལ་བུ་བཞི་དཀར་པོ་བྱུང་། །ཕ་བས་བུ་བཟང་ཕ་མེས་ཀྱི། །ཡུལ་མཁར་ཟིན་པ་འོང་བ་ཡིན། །བུ་བཞི་ནག་བྱུང་བྱིས་པ་ནི། །གསོ་དཀའ་མགོ་མི་ཐོན་པ་འབྱུང་། །འདྲེས་མ་ཕྱེད་སོས་ཕྱེད་མི་སོས། །གྲོགས་བཞི་དཀར་ན་ཁ་རྗེ་དང་། །ཟ་ཐང་ཆེ་ལ་སྟག་རིས་འདྲ། །གྲོགས་བཞི་ནག་ན་དར་གུད་ནོར། །གང་སྟོངས་ཆེ་འོང་། འདྲེས་མ་དེ། །ཡོད་མི་བདེན་ལ་མེད་པ་འོང་། །མི་བདེན་ནོར་རྫས་འབྲིང་དུ་འོང་། །དེ་ཡང་སྲོག་རང་དར་བ་ལ། །དགྲ་གུད་བཟང་སྟེ་དེ་ལྡོག་ངན། །དབང་ཀླུང་རྣམས་ཀྱང་དེ་བཞིན་ཡིན། །སྲོག་གུད་ཆགས་ཆེ་སྲོག་གི་དགྲ། །དར་ལ་སྐྱེས་ན་ཐ་གྲིར་འཆི། །དེ་ཡི་རིགས་པས་གཞན་རྣམས་ཤེས།།» The four friends: white,
standing and a great share, like tiger stripes; black, wealth up and
down, great losses; mixed, what is there is not real, what is not comes,
wealth middling. Then the rule of own against enemy: the life force
rising with its enemy declining is good, the reverse bad, the power and
luck alike; the life force declining with its enemy rising, death by the
blade at last. The notes (p. 484, img. 492, on the scan): «མ་བཞི་ཞེས་པ་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་གི་མ་བཞིའོ།», the four mothers are the mother
pebbles of the life force, body, power and luck: the sets are taken
across the four aspects, one kind of pebble at a time. P. 349 reads as
the etext, and goes on: «འོན་ཀྱང་བཟང་འབྲས་གང་འབྱུང་ཡང་། །འདིར་བརྗོད་པ་ལས་ཤེས་པ་དང་། །དེ་བཞིན་ངན་པར་གྱུར་ན་ཡང་། །ལས་རྩིས་བརྗོད་ལས་བསླུ་མི་སྲིད། །དེ་ཕྱིར་ཤེས་རབ་རིག་པ་ཡིས། །ཡས་མས་ཆ་བསྡུར་ཞིབ་བརྟགས་ཏེ། །ཁ་དམར་མ་ནོར་ཚུལ་བཞིན་སྨྲོས།»: whatever
good comes is known from what is told here, and if it turns bad, the
reckoning of karma does not deceive; so compare above and below
closely and speak the verdict without error. The rule for the whole
stands as read from the etext: neither one nor two results decide; the
good and the bad weighed by force and number throughout, good if the
good are greater and more, bad if the bad, middling if even; the rites
of the moment can turn the bad, passing conditions the good.

**What the pages give for each step of the ladder.**

| Step | Found, with its page |
| --- | --- |
| 2, the verdict of a part | The hundred pebbles' verdicts, each of the twenty-five (life force, body, power, luck, and the mewa or element, each own, mother, son, friend and enemy) white or black with its own reading (pp. 329–331); the enemy pebble reads the other way, black the good one. "Gather it well and state the verdict without error" (p. 335) |
| 3, sets | Twenty joined readings that name several pebbles of several aspects with the trigram and mewa together (pp. 334–335); the four mothers, the four sons, the four friends, many white or many black over the four aspects, and own against enemy (p. 338). A set outweighs the single readings: "nine goods told turn to nine ills", "told good, it goes wrong", "whatever bad times are told, senses whole" |
| 4, which part of life | (The board, from KD vol. 3, pp. 716–718: [claims/birth-hundred-board.md](claims/birth-hundred-board.md).) The general verdict of the hundred (རྡེའུ་སྤྱི་ཡི་ཁ་དམར, rde'u spyi yi kha dmar, p. 331): read from where white and black lie, white making the four continents above the first part of life, in the middle power and wealth, below old age; black likewise; images by the layout (white in a row where the sons lie, the corners black, white above and black below). The domains of each of the twenty-five (p. 334): mother "the coat at one's back", son "the staff in front", friend "the fold of fortune", enemy "an iron she-wolf" |
| 5, the whole | The twenty-one children (pp. 338–340) are kinds a birth falls into, good, middling or bad, each named by its conditions together, without a count; the groups' results (p. 340). The close (p. 349): weigh all, the rites can turn it. The notes (p. 489, on the scan): the child's own luck by the parents' years "is the sum of what was shown above; there is nothing to reckon apart", and the planets' and mansions' life reckoning of the Kālacakra and the *Arising of the Vowels* is good to examine too |
| Conditions | The luck rules the rest: if the luck's tomb is not known, help and harm do not fit; "the luck is chief among conditions and turns it"; the birth luck does not change, the obstacle reckoning's rise and decline does, applied to month, day and hour (p. 335; the notes, p. 484) |

**The twenty-one children: which conditions are dates.** Of the
twenty-one, the calendar alone gives: the weekday and mansion (good 1,
bad 1, bad 7), the waxing half (good 1, good 2), the full moon, the 15th
(good 2, by the notes), the dates 11–20 (good 7, by the notes), the part
of the day: dusk or morning (good 1), sunrise (good 7), midnight or noon
(bad 5), the hour (bad 7); the month against the child's own life force
(bad 5, its seventh; bad 7), a day of the four slayers (bad 5), an
eclipse or a bad earth lord's moving (good 5). Most of the rest needs the
parents: the year and month against the mother's or father's life force,
the mother's obstacle reckoning at the birth (the notes, p. 484: the
whole round is reckoned as the mother's obstacle reckoning, the child
good when born at a time good for the mother), the father's clan. Good 3,
good 6, middle 1, middle 2 and middle 7 are observations or events
(dreams, signs at birth, the family living, conception against
medicine), and good 6 says so itself: "no reckoning is needed, look to
his karma and luck". No child is defined by a date alone; each joins
several conditions, and no rule says how many must hold.

### Open

- Open question 184, answered 2026-10-22: the board is five rows of twenty, the five unchanging lucks against the four aspects' own, mother, son, friend and enemy, the twenty deciding pebbles below ([claims/birth-hundred-board.md](claims/birth-hundred-board.md), from KD vol. 3, pp. 716–718 on the lent scan).
- Open question 185, answered 2026-10-22 as far as the sources go: no text counts a child's conditions; each child is named by its conditions together ([claims/birth-children-no-count.md](claims/birth-children-no-count.md)).

### Searched and not found

- A count or weight for the twenty-one children, or a rule for when two
  children's conditions both hold (pp. 338–340, the notes pp. 484–485, on
  the scan): none.
- The same in the manuals and BDRC's full text, 2026-10-22: Lha mo
  tshul khrims (LT, printed pp. 158, 167) restates the three groups'
  results and sends the rest to WB; the *Utpala Garland* (UT) and the
  Bhutan collection (I1KG11905) do not treat the children; BDRC's search
  for «རང་བཞིན་ཆགས་པའི་བུ», «ལས་ཅན་རིགས་རྙེད», «བསོད་ནམས་ལྷུན་གྲུབ་བུ», «བཤན་པ་བདུད་འབེབས»,
  «འབྱུང་བ་གསོ་བསྐྱེད», «མདུང་ཤུལ་སྲིན་མོ» finds WB's prints, LT and Gyurme Dorje only.
- A rule ranking the sets of pp. 334–335 and p. 338 against the hundred's
  single readings, beyond the sets' own wording: none in ch. 22 or the
  notes' section 1.


## D16: the worked examples (O17)

Read 2026-10-20. Two worked examples of the year of age's predictive
pebble, each cell traced to the meeting it records and each verdict
counted again with a mixed place counted four ways. Mipham's (མི་ཕམ, mi
pham) table at the foot of the notes' p. 489 was read cell by cell on the
Gser thang reprint (I3CN5029 img. 495, at 1× and 3×) and checked on the 1996
scan (I1KG12907 img. 497, at 3×); RD20's transcription
([year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md), *The notes' worked example*) stands
in every cell. Gyurme Dorje's (འགྱུར་མེད་རྡོ་རྗེ, 'gyur med rdo rje)
chart 6.2 (*Tibetan Elemental Divination Paintings*, 2001, p. 228) was
not read again: its cells are RD18's reading of archive.org's page image
(above, *What a tie lays*). His text around the chart (pp. 226–230) was
not read either; that is O18's row. WB's son-meeting line (vol. 1, p. 380,
img. 390, l. 25) was read again on the 1996 scan at 4× and 9×. No model
was run: the cells come from arithmetic on the rules already read, and the
decisive cells from the eye.

### The rules each cell follows

- **The six signs and the four aspects** (the notes, vol. 2, p. 490,
  img. 498, l. 1–5, quoted under RD20): the current year, the progressed
  sign (ལོག་མིན, log min), the trigram (སྤར་ཁ, spar kha) of the year of
  age, the mewa (སྨེ་བ, sme ba) of the year of age, the sectors (དར་གུད,
  dar gud) and the hour. Against each of the six, the one reckoned's
  vitality (སྲོག, srog), body (ལུས, lus), power (དབང་ཐང, dbang thang;
  Dorje's "destiny") and luck (ཀླུང་རྟ, klung rta) fight: the year's,
  progressed sign's and hour's four aspects one to one, the trigram's and
  mewa's single element against all four, and the sector of each aspect's
  element at the current year's animal.
- **The pebbles of a meeting** (WB vol. 1, p. 380, img. 390, O10d's
  reading in [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)): the mother three
  white, the friend two white, the son one white and one black, the enemy
  two black; meeting its own element, earth and water one white, wood,
  iron and fire one black. The son's line, read again on the scan:

  > །བུ་ཕྲད་དཀར་ན་གཟུང་དུ་བོར།

  The syllable dot (ཚེག, tsheg) falls after «ན» and the next word is «གཟུང»: the print does
  not say «དཀར་ནག་ཟུང», "white and black as a pair", in letters. Taken
  literally, it says "a son meeting: where white, lay it as a pair". Both
  examples print every son meeting as ○×, one white and one black, so the
  pebbles of a son meeting are not in doubt. Only the wording is.
- **The kinships** (WB vol. 1, p. 258, img. 268, in
  [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md), *The sectors of growth and
  decline*): wood's mother water and son fire, and so round the five.
- **The sectors' pebbles** (the *Moonbeams*' ranks, KD vol. 3, pp. 496,
  507, etext, in [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)): flourishing and
  working three white, bathing and dressing two, body complete and birth
  one; the breath-taking (དབུགས་ལེན, dbugs len) and the womb (མངལ་གནས,
  mngal gnas) one white and one black; decline and illness one black,
  death and the tomb two black. Every sector cell of both examples
  follows these ranks: the notes' bathing ○○ and flourishing ○○○, Dorje's
  birth ○ and illness ×. WB's own grading of the twelve on p. 335 (birth
  middling, bathing the last of the good) would give other counts. In both
  orders the breath-taking and the womb are "the best of the bad", the
  sectors' mixed pair.

So a place can be mixed in two ways only: a son meeting (any of the six
signs), or an aspect standing in the breath-taking or the womb (the
sectors' column).

### The notes' table, cell by cell (vol. 2, p. 489, img. 497)

A man of an iron dragon year in his sixth year (the wood bird year; the
figure under the heading is ༦, not the row of the la's (བླ, bla) ༩), his natal mewa nine,
red, fire. By WB's rules his progressed sign from the water tiger is the
fire sheep, his trigram gin (གིན, gin), earth, his mewa of the year of
age two, black, water, the current year's mewa four, green, wood. The
heading row prints all of these («བརྩི་བྱ་ལྕགས་འབྲུག», «གནམ་ལོ་ཤིང་བྱ།», «ལོག་མིན་མེ་ལུག», «སྤར་ཁ་ས་གིན།», «བབ་སྨེ་ཆུ་གཉིས་ནག», «དུས་ཚོད་ས་འབྲུག», «བཏང་གཅོད།»).
His aspects are vitality earth, body iron, power iron and luck wood
(«སྲོག་ ས།», «ལུས་ ལྕགས།», «དབང་ ལྕགས།», «ཀླུང་ ཤིང་།»). The wood bird
year's are iron, water, wood and water. The fire sheep's are earth, water,
fire and fire. The earth dragon hour's are earth, wood, earth and wood.
Each cell gives the other element, the meeting and the printed pebbles:

| Aspect | Year | Progressed sign | Trigram | Mewa | Sector | Hour | Printed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vitality, earth | iron, **son** ○× | earth, own ○ | earth, own ○ | water, friend ○○ | bird: bathing ○○ | earth, own ○ | ○ |
| Body, iron | water, **son** ○× | water, **son** ○× | earth, mother ○○○ | water, **son** ○× | bird: flourishing ○○○ | wood, friend ○○ | ○ |
| Power, iron | wood, friend ○○ | fire, enemy ×× | earth, mother ○○○ | water, **son** ○× | bird: flourishing ○○○ | earth, mother ○○○ | ○ |
| Luck, wood | water, mother ○○○ | fire, **son** ○× | earth, friend ○○ | water, mother ○○○ | bird: **womb** ○× | wood, own × | ○ |
| La, fire (mewa 9) | mewa 4 wood, mother ○○○ | mewa 9 fire, own × | gin earth, **son** ○× | mewa 2 water, enemy ×× | bird: death ×× | mewa 2 water, enemy ×× | × |

Every derived cell is the printed one. The la's progressed-sign
and hour mewa (9 and 2) are taken as printed: the notes' rule for the
mewa line that matches the one reckoned's is not worked out here. The
vitality's hour cell carries a speck below its white pebble on the
reprint, absent from the 1996 scan: one white.

Mixed places: six son meetings and one womb, as follows:

- the vitality's year;
- the body's year, progressed sign and mewa;
- the power's mewa;
- the luck's progressed sign, and its sector, the womb;
- the la's trigram.

The lead in the row holds, with one correction: the body's three ○× are
all son meetings, but of the luck's two, one is a son meeting and the
other the womb.

### Dorje's chart 6.2, cell by cell (p. 228)

A man of a fire dragon year in his 23rd year (the earth tiger year 1998),
at a water snake hour. His natal mewa is six, white, and his mewa of the
year of age seven, red, fire. His progressed sign is the iron mouse and
his trigram zin (ཟིན, zin), wood. His aspects are vitality earth, body
earth, power fire and luck wood. The earth tiger's are wood, earth, earth
and iron. The iron mouse's are water, earth, iron and wood. The water
snake's are fire, water, water and water.

| Aspect | Year | Progressed sign | Trigram | Mewa | Sector | Hour | Printed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vitality, earth | wood, enemy ×× | water, friend ○○ | wood, enemy ×× | fire, mother ○○○ | tiger: illness × | fire, mother ○○○ | ○× |
| Body, earth | earth, own ○ | earth, own ○ | wood, enemy ×× | fire, mother ○○○ | tiger: illness × | water, friend ○○ | ○ |
| Power, fire | earth, **son** ○× | iron, friend ○○ | wood, mother ○○○ | fire, own × | tiger: birth ○ | water, enemy ×× | ○× |
| Luck, wood | iron, enemy ×× | wood, own × | wood, own × | fire, **son** ○× | tiger: working ○○○ | water, mother ○○○ | × |

Every derived cell is RD18's reading of the chart. Mixed places: two son
meetings, the power's year and the luck's mewa; no aspect stands in the
breath-taking or the womb.

### The predictive pebbles counted again

Each place counts once (the notes, p. 490). A pure place counts by its
colour. A mixed place counts (a) black, (b) white, (c) not at all, or (d)
as one white and one black. More white lays a white pebble, more black a
black one, and a tie a speckled one (ཁྲ་བོ, khra bo). The count is given
as white : black. "Pebbles" is the count by single pebbles, which p. 381
and the notes reject; it is shown for comparison.

| Example | Aspect | Pure places ○ : × | Mixed | (a) black | (b) white | (c) left out | (d) one of each | Pebbles | Printed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Notes p. 489 | Vitality | 5 : 0 | 1 son | ○ 5:1 | ○ 6:0 | ○ 5:0 | ○ 6:1 | ○ 8:1 | ○ |
| Notes p. 489 | Body | 3 : 0 | 3 son | **speckled 3:3** | ○ 6:0 | ○ 3:0 | ○ 6:3 | ○ 11:3 | ○ |
| Notes p. 489 | Power | 4 : 1 | 1 son | ○ 4:2 | ○ 5:1 | ○ 4:1 | ○ 5:2 | ○ 12:3 | ○ |
| Notes p. 489 | Luck | 3 : 1 | 1 son, 1 womb | **speckled 3:3** | ○ 5:1 | ○ 3:1 | ○ 5:3 | ○ 10:3 | ○ |
| Notes p. 489 | La | 1 : 4 | 1 son | × 1:5 | × 2:4 | × 1:4 | × 2:5 | × 4:8 | × |
| Dorje 6.2 | Vitality | 3 : 3 | — | speckled 3:3 | speckled 3:3 | speckled 3:3 | speckled 3:3 | **○ 8:5** | ○× |
| Dorje 6.2 | Body | 4 : 2 | — | ○ 4:2 | ○ 4:2 | ○ 4:2 | ○ 4:2 | ○ 7:3 | ○ |
| Dorje 6.2 | Power | 3 : 2 | 1 son | speckled 3:3 | **○ 4:2** | **○ 3:2** | **○ 4:3** | **○ 7:4** | ○× |
| Dorje 6.2 | Luck | 2 : 3 | 1 son | × 2:4 | **speckled 3:3** | × 2:3 | × 3:4 | **○ 7:5** | × |

The bold cells are the ones that contradict the printed verdict.

- **The notes' table** is reproduced by (b), (c) and (d), and by the
  count by single pebbles. It is not reproduced by (a): counted black,
  the body's three son meetings tie with its three white places and would
  lay a speckled pebble, where the notes print a white one. The body row
  alone decides this: its only mixed places are son meetings. The luck
  row decides less. With the son meeting black and the womb white or left
  out, it still comes to white (4:2, 3:2); only (a) applied to both its
  mixed places ties it.
- **Dorje's chart** is reproduced by (a) only. (b), (c) and (d) lay a white
  pebble on the power, where he prints ○×; (b) also ties the luck, where
  he prints ×. The count by single pebbles fails on three aspects.
- **No single rule reproduces both.** Let a son meeting add w to the white
  and b to the black, whatever the numbers. The notes' body (three white
  places, three sons) is white only if 3 + 3w > 3b, that is b < w + 1.
  Dorje's power (three white, two black, one son) ties only if 3 + w =
  2 + b, that is b = w + 1. The two cannot both hold. The womb and the
  breath-taking do not enter this argument: neither example's verdict
  turns on them alone.
- **The la** comes out black under every count, as printed. The notes'
  deciding column holds only white and black: the table shows no
  speckled pebble.
- **The body's exception does not change either example.** WB's
  «ཤིང་གཅིག་ས་ལ་མི་གནོད», "one wood does not harm earth" (p. 380), and
  the notes' «ལུས་སྐབས་དགྲ་འཕྲད་ཀྱང་མི་གནོད་པ», "in the body's case even
  an enemy meeting does no harm" (p. 490), touch only Dorje's body
  (earth against the trigram zin's wood, ××). Counted as harmless or
  left out, the body is white either way.

So the two worked examples disagree on the son meeting. Mipham's own
table counts it as not black: as white, as one of each, or left out; the
table cannot tell these three apart. Dorje's chart counts it black. At
this point his text quotes the *Moonbeams* (f. 28b; RD18, above), and
the notes are WB's own reading. Which of the two is the tradition's rule,
or whether one table is in error, the examples do not say. Neither
example contains a verdict that turns on the breath-taking or the womb
alone.

### Open

- Open question 186 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): how a son meeting counts, the notes' table against Dorje's chart.
- Open question 187 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): p. 380's «བུ་ཕྲད་དཀར་ན་གཟུང་དུ་བོར».
- Doubt, not a question: Dorje's predictive column (○× on the vitality and
  the destiny) is RD18's reading of the page image. Whether he means one
  speckled pebble or a white and a black laid together, and how his text
  on pp. 226–230 counts a son meeting, were not read here: the page images
  could not be fetched in this reading. O18 reads that text.

### Searched and not found

- 2026-10-20, WB vol. 1, p. 380 (img. 390, l. 25), on the 1996 scan at
  4× and 9×: no «ནག» after «དཀར» in the son-meeting line; the print reads
  «དཀར་ན་གཟུང».
- 2026-10-20, the notes' table, p. 489 (both prints): no cell whose
  meeting is a breath-taking stage, and no row whose verdict turns on the
  womb alone.



## D16: the Moonbeams (O18)

Read 2026-10-20 in the *Moonbeams* (ཟླ་བའི་འོད་ཟེར, zla ba'i 'od zer),
Lochen Dharmaśrī's (ལོ་ཆེན་དྷརྨ་ཤྲཱི, lo chen dharma shrI) *'Byung rtsis man
ngag zla ba'i 'od zer*, on BDRC's open scan of his *Gsung 'bum*, vol. 5
(I2533, DZ in [README.md](README.md); a clean typeset print, 4800 px, six
or seven lines a side). Its own page numbers stand in the margins: img. 046
carries "21", img. 047 "22", img. 060 "28"; folio N a is img. 2N + 3 and
N b img. 2N + 4 (f. 15a = img. 033, as [reckonings.md](reckonings.md) has
it). Yigdzin-1 over img. 015–064, MITRA as witness over img. 026, 034–036,
046–047, 049, 051, 056–057, 060–061 (99–100 % of syllables agreed; of
its eleven flags only img. 026's «མངལ་གསན», Yigdzin-1's slip for the scan's
«མངལ་གནས», touches a quoted line), and every line quoted below read on the
scan in half-line crops at native size; the name and title from KD's
title line, «ལོ་ཆེན་དྷརྨ་ཤྲཱིས་མཛད» (etext p. 506), and the colophon, «བན་ཆུང་དྷརྨ་ཤྲཱི» (img. 063, Yigdzin-1 only).
KD vol. 3's etext (VEIE0OPI2F246A26_I4717, pp. 516–535) placed the
passages; where it differs from the scan, the scan is given and KD's
reading named. Gyurme Dorje's (འགྱུར་མེད་རྡོ་རྗེ, 'gyur med rdo rje)
*Tibetan Elemental Divination Paintings* (2001), pp. 226 and 228, read on
archive.org's page images, pp. 229–230 in its OCR text (archive.org item
`gyurme-dorje-gyur-med-rdo-rje-tibetan-elemental-divination-paintings-john-eskena`;
a modern author, quoted no further than the facts need).

### Where Dorje's folios fall

Dorje cites the *Moonbeams* by another print's folios. His ff. 27b–28a
(the forty-seven pebbles), 28a (the six root pebbles), 28a/b (the
twenty-four) and 28b (the four verdicts) are this print's f. 21a–22a
(img. 045–047), about six and a half folios earlier. This print's own f. 28b (img. 060) is the reckoning for the
dead, where the tie «མཉམ་ན་ཕྱེད་མ» stands; that line is not the one Dorje
quotes.

### The year of age: the colours and the verdict (f. 21b–22a)

Chapter 5 (its colophon, f. 24a, img. 051, l. 4), which opens «ལོ་གཅིག་མཁྱུད་བསྐོར་ཀེག་རྩིས་ལ།», for the obstacle reckoning of the year's round (f. 21a, img. 045, l. 5; this print has no chapter heading, KD's etext adds one), after the six root pebbles; f. 21b
(img. 046), l. 2 to f. 22a (img. 047), l. 1, read on the scan line by
line:

> དེ་རྗེས་གཅོད་འབྲལ་རྡེའུ་ནི། །བརྩི་བྱའི་སྲོག་གིས་གནམ་ལོ་དང་། །ལོག་མེན་གཉིས་ཀྱི་སྲོག་དང་ནི། །སྤར་སྨེ་ལ་འཐབས་རྡེའུ་བཞི། །དར་གུད་བསྐོར་ཏེ་གནམ་ལོ་ཡི། །སྟེང་དུ་བཟང་ངན་བརྩིས་པས་ལྔ། །དུས་ཚོད་སྲོག་ལ་འཐབས་པས་དྲུག །དེ་རྣམས་སྲོག་རྡེལ་དྲུག་ཡིན་ནོ། །དེ་བཞིན་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་རྟ་བཞིས། །རྩ་བའི་རྡེའུར་འཐབས་པ་དང་། །དར་གུད་གནམ་ལོའི་སྟེང་བརྩིས་ཏེ། །ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་བཞིར་དྲུག་དྲུག་བོར། །དེ་དག་རྡེའུ་འབོར་ཚུལ་ཀྱང་། །མ་འཕྲད་དཀར་གསུམ་གྲོགས་ལ་གཉིས། །ས་ཆུ་ནང་ཕྲད་དཀར་པོ་གཅིག །བུ་ཕྲད་ཕྱེད་མ་ནག་གཉིས་དགྲ། །ཤིང་ལྕགས་མེ་གསུམ་ནག་གཅིག་བོར། །དར་གུད་ལའང་བཟང་དྲུག་གི །རབ་འབྲིང་ཐ་མའི་གོ་རིམ་བཞིན། །དཀར་པོ་གསུམ་དང་གཉིས་དང་གཅིག །ངན་དྲུག་རབ་འབྲིང་ཐ་གསུམ་ལ། །ཕྱེད་མ་ནག་གཅིག་ནག་གཉིས་སོ། །དེ་ནས་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་[f. 22a] བཞི་ཡང་། །དཀར་ནག་མང་ཉུང་བགྲང་བྱས་ལ། །དཀར་གསུམ་དཀར་གཉིས་སོགས་སུ་བོར། །དེ་རྗེས་རྩུབ་པའི་རྡེལ་བདུན་ནི།

Then the twenty-four deciding pebbles: the vitality of the one reckoned
fights the vitality of the present year and of the progressed sign, and
the trigram and mewa (སྨེ་བ, sme ba): four pebbles; with the stages of
growth and decline (དར་གུད, dar gud) turned round onto the present year,
good or bad, five; fighting the hour, six: these are the six pebbles of
the vitality. So too body, power and luck fight the root pebbles, and
with the stages reckoned on the present year, six each are laid for the
four. And the way to lay those pebbles: a meeting with the mother, three
white; with a friend, two; an inner meeting of earth or water, one
white; **a meeting with the son, a half** (ཕྱེད་མ, phyed ma); the enemy,
two black; wood, iron and fire, lay one black. For the stages too, the
six good by the order of best, middling and last: white three, two and
one; the six bad, best, middling and last: **a half**, one black, two
black. Then the four verdict pebbles (ཁ་དམར, kha dmar): counting how many
or few the white and black are, lay them as "three white", "two white"
and so on. Then the seven harsh pebbles …

On the scan: «བུ་ཕྲད་ཕྱེད་མ» (KD's etext «བུ་འཕྲད་པ་བྱེད་མ», a misreading);
«ཐ་གསུམ་ལ» (KD «ཐ་གསུམ་མ»); «བགྲང་བྱས» (KD «བགྲངས་བྱས»). Nothing stands
between «བགྲང་བྱས་ལ» and «དཀར་གསུམ་དཀར་གཉིས་སོགས་སུ་བོར»: the verdict is
these three lines whole, and the next line begins the harsh pebbles. The
verse says nothing of a tie and nothing of how a half is counted.

The order of the stages is f. 11b's (img. 026, l. 3–4, on the scan):
«དར་བ་ལས་བྱེད་བཟང་པོའི་རབ། །ཁྲུས་བྱེད་གོས་གྱོན་བཟང་པོའི་འབྲིང་། །ལུས་རྫོགས་བཙས་པ་བཟང་པོའི་མཐའ། །དབུགས་ལེན་མངལ་གནས་ངན་པའི་རབ། །གུད་པ་ན་བ་ངན་པའི་འབྲིང་། །ཤི་བ་དུར་ཞུགས་ངན་པའི་ཐ།», flourishing and working the
best of the good, bathing and dressing the middling, body complete and
birth the last; the breath-taking (དབུགས་ལེན, dbugs len) and the womb
(མངལ་གནས, mngal gnas) the best of the bad, decline and illness the
middling, death and the tomb the last. So the two stages D16 asks about
are, in the *Moonbeams*, a half each, as the son's meeting is.

**What this settles and what not.**

- The *Moonbeams* names the mixed places' value: the son's meeting and
  the breath-taking and womb stages are each **a half**, one value, set
  in its own place on a scale of six: three white, two white, one white,
  a half, one black, two black. WB's line for the son (vol. 1, p. 380,
  img. 390, in [year-of-life.md](year-of-life.md)) reads «བུ་ཕྲད་དཀར་ན་གཟུང་དུ་བོར།», white and black laid as a pair; the *Moonbeams* says
  the same place in one word.
- It does not say whether a half tips the count of the verdict, is left
  out, or ties. It lays the verdict "counting how many or few the white
  and black are", where WB (p. 381) says "not taking in the threes and
  twos … count the white and black of the faces"; the *Moonbeams* names
  the threes and twos instead, as the forms the verdict is laid in.
- «དཀར་གསུམ་དཀར་གཉིས་སོགས་སུ་བོར» reads most plainly as: the verdict pebble
  itself is laid as a grade of the same scale, "three white", "two white"
  and the rest, so that a verdict can be a half as a place can. Dorje
  reads it otherwise (below): the verdicts placed upon the piles of three
  white, two white and so on. The words bear either; no other line of
  the chapter decides.
- No reading of a half verdict of the year of age is given: the chapter
  gives no readings at all and sends the reader elsewhere. f. 22b
  (img. 048, l. 4–5, on the scan), after the variant of thirty-one or
  thirty-seven pebbles: «དེ་ལྟར་བརྩིས་པའི་འབྲས་བུ་ནི། །རྩ་བའི་འགྲེལ་པ་ཉི་ཤུ་དང་། །དམར་ཆུང་སྟག་མགོ་སྒང་སྒྲིལ་སོགས། །བལྟས་ཏེ་ཉེས་སྐྱོན་བཅོས་པ་གཅེས།», the results so reckoned: look in the root's
  commentary, the twenty, and the little red, tiger-head, rolled-ridge and
  the rest (works named by these words, not identified here), and
  correcting the faults is what matters.
- For the larger variants (f. 24a, img. 051, l. 1–3, on the scan) it
  allows a shortcut: «འོན་ཀྱང་གནས་སྐབས་བྱ་སླའི་ཆེད། །སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་སྨེ་བ་ལྔས། །ཚེ་དང་ནད་དང་ཟ་ཐང་དང་། །བསོད་ནམས་གདོན་བྱད་ལ་བརྟགས་པའི། །ཁ་དམར་སྤྱི་བསྡོམས་ལྔར་བསྡུས་ལ། །རིགས་མཐུན་འབྲས་བུ་གང་རུང་གིས། །སྟངས་གཅོད་བྱས་ཀྱང་ཆོག་པར་ངེས།», yet for ease at the time, the
  verdicts examined for life, illness, food, merit and demons by the
  five, vitality, body, power, luck and mewa, may be gathered into five
  general totals and decided (སྟངས་གཅོད, stangs gcod) by any result of
  the same kind.
