| Vol. 2, p. 263 (img. 271, l. 13–15), geomancy, the shapes of mountains | «…དམངས་སོགས་བྱེ་དྲག་མི་འདྲའི་ཚུལ། །དུ་མའི་བཤད་མང་ཡོད་ནའང་། །བཟང་ངན་གཏང་གཅོད་དབྱེ་དཀའ་ལ། །ཀུན་ཀྱང་ངན་པར་འཁུམས་འགྱུར་ཞིང་། །འབྱམས་ཀླས་ལག་ཏུ་ལེན་དཀའ་བས། །འདིར་ནི་དགོས་དོན་སྙིང་པོ་རུ། །དྲིལ་ཏེ་བཀོད་པ་དེ་ཙམ་མོ། །» | though there are many accounts of the kinds (mountains like commoners and the rest), it is hard to tell good from bad by deciding; all would come to look bad and be endless and hard to use, so only the needful core is gathered here | the Gser thang reprint (img. 269, the same typesetting), on the scan: «གཏང་གཅོད»; **WBZ img. 995, l. 2, on the scan: «བཟང་ངན་བཏང་གཅོད་དབྱེ་དཀའ»**; the *Collected Old Texts*' copy's etext «btang gcod» |

Besides these, the 1996 etext has «བཏང་གཅོད» at vol. 1, p. 238 («ལག་ནི་གཡབ་མོས་བཏང་གཅོད་ཕྱིར», the name *gtsug lag* (གཙུག་ལག, gtsug lag): *lag*
because it decides by beckoning; RD25), p. 245 (a text's name, RD25) and
p. 248 (the *Lamp of the Magic of Deciding*, RD25), and in vol. 2 at
p. 147 (the arrow of decision, RD18) and p. 150 («བཟང་ངན་ཁ་སྨང་གི། །བཏང་གཅོད་དཔྱད་རིགས», the kinds of examination that decide good or
bad), etext only. WB's own lines never write «སྟངས་གཅོད».

**Mipham's notes** (vol. 2, pp. 474–521 in the 1996 etext, img. 482–529)
write «བཏང་གཅོད» at every use (pp. 474–476, 489–490, 495, 498, 500–504,
510, 513), and so does his own print, read on the scan:

- I2029, img. 661, l. 6 (the notes' p. 495): «…ཞེས་བཏང་གཅོད་དཀར་ན་ཆགས» (Yigdzin-1 «རྡེལ་དཔྱད་སྐབས་ཐང་ཤིང་ཚོགས་ཞེས་བཏང་གཅོད་དཀར་ན་ཆགས»; the 1996 «ཆགས་ཞེས»), "standing" means the deciding pebble is white;
- I2029, img. 668, l. 1 (p. 498): «གོང་གི་ཐང་ཤིང་དང་བཏང་གཅོད་ནག་ན་དང་།», the plank-tree (ཐང་ཤིང, thang shing) above and "if the deciding pebble is black".

Sde srid's own *Supplement* (ལྷན་ཐབས, lhan thabs) on the Four Tantras
writes it the same, «འཚོ་འཆི་བཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་གདགས་པ་ནི» (BDRC snippets of two
copies, etext only).

So in WB the spelling is «བཏང་གཅོད» in every print read; the one
«གཏང་གཅོད» (p. 263) is the 1996 typesetting's, which the Zhol blocks do not
have.

### 2. «སྟངས་གཅོད» stands where WB has «བཏང་གཅོད»

The same count, the same rule and the same verses, written with the
other spelling:

| WB («བཏང་གཅོད») | The other text («སྟངས་གཅོད») | Where read |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Vol. 1, p. 260: «རྒྱ་འདྲེའི་རྡེལ་གྲངས་བརྒྱ་ཕྲག་གཅིག །བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེལ་ནི་ཉི་ཤུ་བཞི། །སྤྱིར་བསྡུས་བརྒྱ་དང་སུམ་ཅུ་དྲུག» | *Moonbeams* f. 15b (I2533, img. 034, l. 3): «…བརྒྱ་འདྲེའི་རྡེའུ་བརྒྱ་ཕྲག་གཅིག །སྟངས་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཉི་ཤུ་བཞི། །སྤྱི་བསྡུས་བརྒྱ་དང་སུམ་ཅུ་དྲུག» | DZ on the scan (the deciding word checked again 2026-10-22 at 2×; the line as quoted from this print above); its copies in KD (etext p. 521), the *Treasury of Sūtra and Tantra* (མདོ་རྒྱུད་མཛོད, mdo rgyud mdzod; MW28955_E5A009; its school not checked here) and Nalendra Tshul khrims rgyal mtshan's (ན་ལེནྡྲའི་མཁན་པོ་ཚུལ་ཁྲིམས་རྒྱལ་མཚན, na lendra'i mkhan po tshul khrims rgyal mtshan) writings (MW1KG10375_747F39) the same; the *Great Treasure Good Vase* (གཏེར་ཆེན་བུམ་བཟང, gter chen bum bzang), a word commentary on the same *Treasury* (MW28955_2515DB), «stang gcod rde'u nyer bzhi» (BDRC snippets) |
| Vol. 1, p. 261: «བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཉི་ཤུ་ལ། །སྲོག་གི་རྡེའུ་དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས།» | *Moonbeams* (KD etext p. 522): «…དེ་ལྟར་ངེས་པར་བྱས་ནས་ཀྱང་། །རྡེའུ་ཉེར་བཞིས་སྟངས་གཅོད་དོ། །» | DZ, as quoted above from its print; KD etext |
| Vol. 1, p. 385: «སོ་སོའི་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་གི། །བཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་རེ་དང་།» | Kun bzang blo gros's (ཀུན་བཟང་བློ་གྲོས, kun bzang blo gros) supplement to the *Moonbeams*, the *Garland of Mansions* (རྒྱུ་སྐར་ཕྲེང་བ, rgyu skar phreng ba; KD vol. 3, p. 664, etext p. 684), WB's verses taken over whole: «…བཟའ་ཚང་ཁྱིམ་གཅིག་མི་དུ་ཡོད། །སོ་སོའི་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་གི །སྟངས་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་རེ་དང་། ། སྨེ་བ་ཁམས་རྡེལ་ལྔ་བོར་ལ། །» | KD etext only |
| Vol. 1, p. 468: «བསྲེག་སྒྲུབ་ཀྱི། །བཏང་གཅོད་རྩིས་འགོ་རྣམ་བརྒྱད» | *Moonbeams* ch. 2 (KD p. 500, etext p. 520): «རོ་ཡི་སྲེག་སྒྲུབ་སྟངས་གཅོད་བརྩི།», reckon the deciding of burning or keeping the corpse; Tshul khrims rgyal mtshan's *Mirror Clarifying the Divination Chart of Chinese Reckoning* (རྒྱ་ནག་རྩིས་ཀྱི་གབ་ཙེ་གསལ་བའི་མེ་ལོང, rgya nag rtsis kyi gab tse gsal ba'i me long; MW1KG10408): «མི་རོ་དམར་པོ་ནི་རོ་ཡི་བསྲེག་སྒྲུབ་སྟངས་གཅོད་ཀྱི་རྩིས་གཞི», the red corpse is the reckoning base for deciding burning or keeping | KD etext; BDRC snippet |
| Vol. 2, p. 46: «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེལ། །དཀར་མང་དཀར་ལ་ནག་མང་ནག །ཕྱེད་མ་ཁྲ་བོར་བོར་བ་ཡི།» | a text in the collection of Upper Dokham's (མདོ་ཁམས་སྟོད, mdo khams stod) scholars (MK's, MW1PD152297): «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་སྟངས་གཅོད་རྡེལ། དཀར་མང་དཀར། ནག་མང་ནག ཕྱེད་མར་ཁྲ་བོ་བོར་གསུངས།» | BDRC snippet (search only) |
| Vol. 2, pp. 55–56: «སྲོག་གི་བཏང་གཅོད་ནག་པ་ན། །སྲོག་གི་འགེལ་ཤིང་ཆག་པ་སྟེ།» and the power's and luck's lines | Kun dga' rig 'dzin's sheet (KD p. 729, on the lent scan, RD28): «སྲོག་གི་སྟངས་གཅོད་ནག་པས་ན། །སྲོག་གི་འགིང་ཤིང་ཆག་པ་སྟེ། …། །ཀླུ་རྟའི་སྟངས་གཅོད་ནག་པ་ན།», and the chart's last column headed «སྟངས་གཅོད»; p. 722 «སྟངས་གཅོད་རྡེལ་བཞི།» | RD28, on the scan |
| Vol. 2, p. 70: «སྐྱེ་གནས་བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་དཀར།» | *Moonbeams* f. 29a (I2533, img. 061, l. 2–3, on the scan, O18 above): «དེ་ལྟར་སྟངས་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཡི། །ཁ་དོག་དང་བསྟུན་ཁ་དམར་ཀྱང་།», the verdicts follow the colour of the deciding pebbles (of the rebirth and the rest) | DZ on the scan |

**One work, both spellings.** Kun bzang blo gros's supplement, which
takes WB's verses over whole, writes the household's line with
«སྟངས་གཅོད» (KD p. 664, above) and WB's verses of the dead with
«བཏང་གཅོད» (KD p. 706, etext p. 726): «དང་པོ་སྲོག་གི་རྡེལ་ཚན་འབྲས། །སྲོག་གི་བཏང་གཅོད་ནག་པ་ན། །སྲོག་གི་འགི་ཤིང་ཆོག་པ་སྟེ། …། །སྲོག་གི་བཏང་གཅོད་དཀར་བ་ན། །འཆི་བའི་དུས་ལ་མ་བབས་ཞེས།»
(etext only). Its own words elsewhere have «སྟངས་གཅོད» (pp. 519, 546,
572: «སྟངས་གཅོད་འབྲས་བཤད», «སྟངས་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་འདི་ལྟར་དཔྱད», «དཀར་ནག་གི །སྟངས་གཅོད་མཛད་པའི་ལུགས»), and the past
form «སྟངས་བཅད» stands in the *Moonbeams* (KD pp. 506, 512, «ལེགས་ཉེས་འབྲས་བུས་སྟངས་བཅད་དོ», «འགྲེལ་པས་སྟངས་བཅད་དེ»). Likewise
Tshul khrims rgyal mtshan writes «སྟངས་གཅོད» in his *Mirror* and in his
copy of the *Moonbeams*, while his annotation to the *Moonbeams*' line on
the twenty-four (BDRC snippet, MW1KG10375_747F39) glosses the place with
the other spelling: «…རང་མ་བུ་གྲོགས་རྩིས་ལ་འོག་ཏུ་གཏང་གཅོད་དཀར་ན་བཟང་།», for the
self, mother, son and friend, white deciding pebbles below are good.

**Restatements of the *Moonbeams*' count with WB's spelling.** Lhamo
Tsultrim's manual (I1KG21886, etext p. 35): «…མི་འགྱུར་རྩ་བའི་རྡེལ་ལྔ་དང་། ། རྒྱ་འདྲེའི་རྡེལ་གྲུངས་བརྒྱ་ཕྲག་གཅིག ། བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཉེར་བཞི་སྟེ། །སྤྱི་བསྡུས་བརྒྱུ་དང་སོ་དྲུག་གོ།» (etext only;
«གྲུངས», «བརྒྱུ» the OCR's), the same count in the same words, and the
dead's lines with «བཏང་གཅོད» (etext p. 54); the same lines in a
manual of laying the pebbles in the Geluk (དགེ་ལྡན, dge ldan) masters'
practice (རྒྱ་རྩིས་དགེ་ལྡན་མཁས་པའི་ཕྱག་རྒྱུན་དུ་བསྟར་བའི་རྡེལ་འགྲེམ, rgya rtsis dge ldan mkhas pa'i phyag
rgyun du bstar ba'i rdel 'grem; BDRC's snippet cuts the title) and in the
*Crafts, Reckoning and Drama* (བཟོ་རྩིས་ཟློས་གར, bzo rtsis zlos gar) vol. 2
(BDRC snippets). The Bhutan collection (I1KG11905, etext pp. 491–496)
writes WB's verses of the dead with «བཏང་གཅོད» too. Zhe chen
Rgyal tshab 'Gyur med padma rnam rgyal (ཞེ་ཆེན་རྒྱལ་ཚབ་འགྱུར་མེད་པདྨ་རྣམ་རྒྱལ, zhe chen
rgyal tshab 'gyur med pad+ma rnam rgyal): «རྡེའུ་ཉེར་བཞི་བཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་བཞི།» (snippet).

**A third spelling in the reckoning itself, «གཏང་གཅོད».** The reckoning for
the dead of the *Mirror of Magic* (སྲིད་པ་འཕྲུལ་གྱི་མེ་ལོང, srid pa 'phrul gyi
me long; in the Drukpa (འབྲུག་པ, 'brug pa) *Treasury of Dharma* (ཆོས་མཛོད, chos
mdzod), MW3CN2232_D26879):
«འགྲམས་ལ་གཏང་གཅོད་རྡེལ་ནག་དག་དང་ནི། །ལོ་ཟླ་ཞག་དུས་སྤར་སྨེ་གཟའ་སྐར་དང་།», among the
faults (འགྲམས, 'grams) black deciding pebbles, and those of year, month,
day, hour, trigram, mewa, planet and mansion; the *Good Vase* (above,
in the Bhutan collection; its copy MW30023_79E1C5, which BDRC ascribes to
Si tu dbon Karma nges legs bstan 'dzin (སི་ཏུ་དབོན་ཀརྨ་ངེས་ལེགས་བསྟན་འཛིན, si tu dbon karma
nges legs bstan 'dzin)): «གཏང་གཅོད་འགྲེམ་པས་ང་ལྔའོ།», laying the deciding pebbles
makes fifty-five (WB's fifty-five, vol. 2, p. 46), and «སིལ་བུལ་སྲེག་སྒྲུབ་གཏང་བཅད་པ།»,
the scattered (pebbles) decide burning or keeping (the Bhutan collection,
I1KG11905, etext p. 72, and the copy); BDRC
snippets, etext only.

### 3. The word outside the pebbles

The same verb, "to decide good from bad", is written all these ways
outside the reckoning, often in copies of one line:

- the judge of the dead: «ལས་ཀྱི་རྡེའུ་དཀར་ནག་བཀྲམ་ནས་དགེ་སྡིག་གི་སྟངས་གཅོད་པ་དང་།», the white and black pebbles of karma are spread out and virtue and sin decided (the *Liberation by Hearing in the Bardo* (བར་དོ་ཐོས་གྲོལ, bar do thos grol) and its copy in the *Treasury of Precious Termas* (རིན་ཆེན་གཏེར་མཛོད, rin chen gter mdzod); «དགེ་སྡིག་གི་སྟངས་གཅོད» has 9 hits, all on the Lord of Death); Zhu chen Tshul khrims rin chen (ཞུ་ཆེན་ཚུལ་ཁྲིམས་རིན་ཆེན, zhu chen tshul khrims rin chen) writes the Lord of Death's office «དགེ་སྡིག་གི་སྟོང་གཅོད» in one place and a protector's «ལེགས་ཉེས་སྟངས་གཅོད» in another (BDRC snippets);
- the protector who decides good and bad: «ལེགས་ཉེས་སྟངས་གཅོད་དམ་ཚིག་སྐྱོང»,
  «ལེགས་ཉེས་ཀུན་གྱི་སྟངས་གཅོད་མ», «ལེགས་ཉེས་བཏང་གཅོད་ཕན་གནོད་སྦྱིན», «དགེ་སྡིག་གཉིས་ཀྱི་སྟང་གཅོད་མ» (snippets);
- G.yu thog's (གཡུ་ཐོག, g.yu thog) praise of the physician who sees life and
  death without divination, one line in three places: «འཚོ་འཆི་ལེགས་ཉེས་སྟངས་གཅོད་ཡོད།» in the *Treasury of Precious Termas* and in one volume of a collection of the *Heart Essence of G.yu thog* (གཡུ་ཐོག་སྙིང་ཐིག, g.yu thog snying thig; I4PD2078),
  «འཚོ་འཆི་ལེགས་ཉེས་སྟོང་གཅོད་ཡོད།» in another volume of the same collection (I4PD2077) (snippets);
- the Four Tantras' children's illnesses: «འཚོ་འཆི་བཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར་གདགས» (17 hits), glossed by three commentaries: Skyem pa Tshe dbang (སྐྱེམ་པ་ཚེ་དབང, skyem pa tshe dbang), «འཚོ་འཆི་བཏང་བཅད་ཁ་དམར་གདགས་པ་གསུམ་ཞེས། … འཚོའམ་འཆི་བའི་བཏང་བཅད་དེ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས་པའམ་ཁས་འཆེ་བ», deciding whether he lives or dies, that is, laying the verdict or pronouncing it (*Rgyud bzhi'i rnam bshad*, I4711); an anonymous commentary, «འཚོ་འཆི་བཏང་གཅོད་ནི། འཚོ་འཆི་ཤན་དབྱེ་བའམ», deciding life and death is telling life and death apart (I8LS18171); and Lung rigs bstan dar's (ལུང་རིགས་བསྟན་དར, lung rigs bstan dar) glossary on Sde srid's *Supplement*, «བཏང་གཅོད་ནི་ཤན་ཕྱེད», deciding is telling apart (I1KG6219, I1PD403) (snippets).

### 4. The dictionaries

Of Steinert's 64 dictionaries (Wylie headwords, `locate.py dict`):

- only the *Ocean of Word Treasures* (ཚིག་གཏེར་རྒྱ་མཚོ, tshig gter rgya mtsho)
  has the reckoning's word, under the spelling «གཏང་གཅོད»: «འབྱུང་རྩིས་ཀྱི་ཐ་སྙད་དེ། རེའུ་མིག་མཇུག་གི་བསྡོམས་རྩིས་ཀྱི་རེའུ་མིག་གི་མིང་། ཁ་དམར་དང་དོན་གཅིག», a term of the
  elemental reckoning (འབྱུང་རྩིས, 'byung rtsis): the name of the summing
  row at the end of a chart, one in meaning with the verdict. That is
  the sheets' last row («སྟངས་གཅོད་རྡེལ་བཞི», p. 722) and their last column
  (p. 729);
- no dictionary has «བཏང་གཅོད», «སྟངས་གཅོད» or «སྟང་གཅོད» as a headword;
- «སྟོང་གཅོད» is an old word (རྙིང, rnying), "deciding whether a thing is
  or is not" (the *Great Tibetan-Chinese Dictionary* (བོད་རྒྱ་ཚིག་མཛོད་ཆེན་མོ,
  bod rgya tshig mdzod chen mo) and the *Ocean of Word Treasures*), from
  A lag sha Bstan dar's (ཨ་ལག་ཤ་བསྟན་དར, a lag sha bstan dar) word list,
  the *Throat Ornament of the Clear-Minded* (བརྡའ་ཡིག་བློ་གསལ་མགྲིན་རྒྱན, brda' yig
  blo gsal mgrin rgyan), whose line BDRC holds (I4CZ45291):
  «སྟངས་འཛིན་དང་སྟོང་གཅོད་ནི་ཡིན་མིན་གྱི་དོན་ཐག་བཅད་པའོ།», *stangs 'dzin* and *stong
  gcod* mean deciding whether a thing is or is not;
- Sgom sde's dictionary (སྒོམ་སྡེ་ཚིག་མཛོད་ཆེན་མོ, sgom sde tshig mdzod chen mo), under
  «སྟངས་འཛིན», reports the pair «སྟངས» and «བཏང» disputed in a neighbouring
  word: Zam gdong Rin po che (ཟམ་གདོང་རིན་པོ་ཆེ, zam gdong rin po che) held
  «སྟངས་འཛིན» a spread misspelling of «བཏང་འཛིན», and the dictionary rejects
  that, deriving «སྟངས» from "manner" (*za stangs*, the way of eating). It
  says nothing of «སྟངས་གཅོད».

Gyurme Dorje's English edition of WB's paintings (MW1KG6061, under
copyright, search only) renders «stangs-gcod rde'u» (from the
*Moonbeams*) and «btang-gcod rde'u» (from WB) the same way, and
transliterates captions of the rebirth's pebbles as «gtang-gcod»; the
snippets do not show which captions.

### The answer

**Answered: yes.** «སྟངས་གཅོད» is WB's «བཏང་གཅོད», the same word for the
same thing, the deciding pebble laid by counting white against black.
Three things show it. (1) The *Moonbeams*, its copies, Kun bzang blo
gros's supplement and the sheets put «སྟངས་གཅོད» in the very places where
WB has «བཏང་གཅོད»: the twenty-four of the 136-pebble reckoning (WB vol. 1,
p. 260; DZ f. 15b), the household's verdict pebble (vol. 1, p. 385; KD
p. 664), burning or keeping the corpse (vol. 1, p. 468; KD p. 500), the
rule for white, black and speckled (vol. 2, p. 46; MK), and the four
verses of the dead (vol. 2, pp. 55–56; KD p. 729). (2) The spellings
cross between copies of one line: the *Moonbeams*' count appears with
«སྟངས», «སྟང» and «བཏང» (DZ, the *Great Treasure Good Vase*, Lhamo Tsultrim);
one supplement and one annotator use two spellings each; and outside
the reckoning one line of G.yu thog's has «སྟངས» and «སྟོང». (3) The only
dictionary that has the reckoning's word («གཏང་གཅོད») makes it the summing
row of a chart, one with the verdict, which is the sheets' row and
column. The spelling divides by line of transmission: WB, its Zhol,
Sde dge and Gser thang prints, Mipham's notes, Sde srid's *Supplement*,
Lhamo Tsultrim and the Bhutan collection's verses of the dead write
«བཏང»; Dharmaśrī's
*Moonbeams*, its supplement, the KD sheets and Tshul khrims rgyal mtshan
write «སྟངས»; «གཏང» stands in the *Mirror of Magic*, the *Good Vase*, the
*Ocean of Word Treasures* and the 1996 WB's p. 263 (a typesetting's: the
Zhol blocks have «བཏང»). **Not settled:** which spelling is the original;
no source states the two to be one word or calls one a misspelling of
the other, and no dictionary has «སྟངས་གཅོད» or «བཏང་གཅོད» at all. WB's
«བཏང་གཅོད» stays the name in these files; the sheets' «སྟངས་གཅོད» is the
same pebble.

### Open

- Open question 247 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): which spelling of the deciding is the older, «བཏང་གཅོད» (WB and
  its prints, Mipham), «སྟངས་གཅོད» (Dharmaśrī's *Moonbeams* and its
  followers), «གཏང་གཅོད» (the *Mirror of Magic*, the *Good Vase*, the
  *Ocean of Word Treasures*) or the old «སྟོང་གཅོད» that the word lists give
  for deciding is or is not; and is «སྟངས» here the "manner" word or a
  sound-spelling of «བཏང»?
- Open question 248 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): is «སྟངས་གཅོད» Dharmaśrī's own spelling, or only that of the
  print of his collected works read here (I2533) and the copies taken
  from it? An older manuscript of the *Moonbeams*, if one survives, would
  show it.
- Doubt, not read on a print: the Taikhang reproduction (I5878) of
  vol. 2, p. 263, against the 1996 «གཏང» and the Zhol «བཏང» (not fetched;
  the Zhol print already differs from the typesetting).

### Searched and not found

- 2026-10-22, Steinert's 64 dictionaries (`locate.py dict`, Wylie
  headwords): «btang gcod», «stangs gcod», «stang gcod», «btangs gcod»,
  «stangs bcad», «btang bcad» no headword; «gtang gcod» only in the
  *Ocean of Word Treasures*; «thag gcod» in seven, none tied to the
  reckoning.
- 2026-10-22, BDRC's full-text search, exact phrases, no hit:
  «གཡབ་མོས་སྟངས་གཅོད», «སྟངས་གཅོད་ཉེར་བཞི», «བཏང་གཅོད་ཉེར་བཞི», «འཚོ་འཆི་སྟངས་གཅོད»,
  «དགེ་སྡིག་གི་བཏང་གཅོད», «གཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར», «སྟངས་གཅོད་ནི», «གཏང་གཅོད་ནི»,
  «བཏང་གཅོད་ཅེས», «སྟངས་གཅོད་ཅེས», «བསྲེག་སྒྲུབ་བཏང་གཅོད», «བསྲེག་སྒྲུབ་གཏང་བཅད»,
  «སྟངས་གཅོད་དཀར་ན་ཆགས»: no gloss of «སྟངས་གཅོད» anywhere, and no text that names
  the two spellings together. Hits counted but not all read (first page
  of results only): «སྟངས་གཅོད» 90, «བཏང་གཅོད» 69, «སྟོང་གཅོད» 43 (most of them
  «རྡུལ་ཕྲན་སྟོང་གཅོད», cutting a thousand atoms, another phrase), «སྟངས་བཅད» 23
  (mostly "posture taken", «འདུག་སྟངས་བཅད»), «བཏང་བཅད» 16 (mostly medical
  recipes, "given, it stops", another phrase), «གཏང་གཅོད» 12, «སྟང་གཅོད» 10,
  «བཏང་གཅོད་ཁ་དམར» 17 (the Four Tantras and their commentaries),
  «བཏངས་གཅོད» 1 (Gyurme Dorje's book only); «སྟང་གཅོད» outside the
  reckoning mostly political («ཞང་བློན་སྟང་གཅོད», ministers' rulings).
- 2026-10-22, the etexts: WB's 1996 etext has no «སྟངས་གཅོད», «སྟང་གཅོད»,
  «བཏངས་གཅོད» or «སྟངས་བཅད» in either volume; the Zhol, second Zhol and
  Sde dge etexts none either (poor OCR). KD vol. 3's etext has
  «སྟངས་གཅོད» only in the *Moonbeams*, Kun bzang blo gros's supplement and
  the sheets, and «བཏང་གཅོད» only in the supplement's verses of the dead
  (pp. 706–707). Dpal bsdus's *'Byung rtsis dpyad don rmad byung* (I4767)
  has «བཏང་གཅོད» once (etext p. 216) and «རྣོ་སྟངས་གཅོད», a sharp deciding, twice
  (pp. 232, 307), no gloss. The *Removing the Tarnish* (I5876) etext: no «སྟངས་གཅོད» and no
  «བཏང་གཅོད» under that OCR (RD18 found «བཏང་གཅོད» once on its poor p. 16).
- 2026-10-22, Mipham's own print (I2029, img. 656–676, Yigdzin-1): no
  «སྟངས་གཅོད».


## Kun dga' rig 'dzin, the writer of the sheets (open question 246)

Read 2026-10-22. BDRC's catalogue records (`ldspdi.bdrc.io/resource/…`)
of the sheets, of KD and of every part of KD's outline (439 nodes walked),
its person search, KD's etext, all five volumes (IE0OPI2F246A26,
I4715–I4719), BDRC's full-text search (`bdrc_search.py`, both result
pages of the bare name, then chained phrases for the search-only hits),
the open etexts of two works that name him, and the national register of
intangible heritage of the People's Republic of China (ihchina.cn, read in
a headed browser). On the scan: KD vol. 3's board of editors (front
matter, BDRC I4717 img. 5, one of the restricted volume's open preview
images) and its contents page (p. 7, img. 13), each line read by eye at
3×; the title leaf's «ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་གྱིས་བྲིས།» was read on the lent
scans by RD28 (p. 713, leaf 732). Everything else is etext or catalogue,
marked so. He is a modern author, perhaps living: what follows is what the
publications state, quoted no further than the facts need.

### 1. The catalogue

The sheets' record, MW28845_B881BE (work WA0XLB881BE5C3B6D), carries the
authorship statement «ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་གྱིས་བྲིས།» (catalogue), "written (drawn) by
Kun dga' rig 'dzin (ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན, kun dga' rig 'dzin)", and **no
link to a person record**: neither the instance nor the work has a
creator. KD's only creator link is the compiler (ཕྱོགས་སྒྲིག་པ, phyogs
sgrig pa, role R0ER0015) Byams pa 'phrin las (བྱམས་པ་འཕྲིན་ལས, byams pa
'phrin las), P4853, 1928–2011. BDRC has **no person record of the name**
(below, *Searched and not found*). Walking KD's whole outline, the name
stands in four authorship statements, all in vols. 2–3, three of them
shared with Phur bu don grub (ཕུར་བུ་དོན་གྲུབ, phur bu don grub):

| Part | Where | Authorship statement (catalogue) | What he did |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

of eclipses, *The Powerful King that Fulfils Wishes* (བསམ་འཕེལ་དབང་རྒྱལ, bsam 'phel
dbang rgyal), section 19 (MW28845_4E5192), KD vol. 2, etext p. 617 =
p. 597, etext only:

> རྒྱ་རྩིས་བསམ་འཕེལ་དབང་རྒྱལ་རབ་རྒྱལ་་མེ་ཡོས་ལོ་ནས་རབ་བྱུང་བཅུ་བདུན་པའི་མེ་ཡོས་ལོར་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་དང་། ཕུར་བུ་དོན་གྲུབ་ཀྱིས་རྩིས་འགོ་སྨོས་པའོ། །

The Chinese reckoning *The Powerful King that Fulfils Wishes*: its epoch moved by Kun
dga' rig 'dzin and Phur bu don grub from the fire hare year of «རབ་རྒྱལ»
to the fire hare year of the 17th sixty-year cycle (རབ་བྱུང, rab byung).
(«སྨོས» for «སྤོས» as the etext has it.)

### 2. KD itself: the board of editors and his own works

**The board.** Every volume of KD prints its editors with their
institutions on a front-matter page (etext p. 5 of vols. 1, 3, 4, 5; p. 6
of vol. 2). Vol. 3's, on the scan (BDRC I4717 img. 5), the lines that
concern him:

> རྩོམ་སྒྲིག་པ་གཙོ་བོ། བྱམས་པ་འཕྲིན་ལས་(བོད་ལྗོངས་སྨན་རྩིས་ཁང་)། རྩོམ་སྒྲིག་པ་གཙོ་བོ་གཞོན་པ། ཁྲོ་རུ་ཚེ་རྣམ་(བོད་ལྗོངས་གསོ་རིག་སློབ་གྲྭ་ཆེན་མོ)། ཚུལ་ཁྲིམས་རྒྱལ་མཚན་(བོད་ལྗོངས་སྨན་རྩིས་ཁང་)། […] རྩོམ་སྒྲིག་པ། […] ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན(བོད་ལྗོངས་གསོ་རིག་སློབ་གྲྭ་ཆེན་མོ)། […] ཕུར་བུ་དོན་གྲུབ(མངའ་རིས་སྨན་རྩིས་ཁང་)། […]

Chief editor (རྩོམ་སྒྲིག་པ་གཙོ་བོ, rtsom sgrig pa gtso bo): Byams pa
'phrin las (of the Tibet Autonomous Region's Mentsikhang (བོད་ལྗོངས་སྨན་རྩིས་ཁང,
bod ljongs sman rtsis khang)). Deputy chief editors: Khro ru Tshe rnam
(ཁྲོ་རུ་ཚེ་རྣམ, khro ru tshe rnam; of the Tibet Autonomous Region's
Tibetan Medical College (བོད་ལྗོངས་གསོ་རིག་སློབ་གྲྭ་ཆེན་མོ, bod ljongs gso
rig slob grwa chen mo)) and Tshul khrims rgyal mtshan (ཚུལ་ཁྲིམས་རྒྱལ་མཚན,
tshul khrims rgyal mtshan; of the Mentsikhang). Editors (རྩོམ་སྒྲིག་པ,
rtsom sgrig pa), eighteen, among them **Kun dga' rig 'dzin, of the Tibet
Autonomous Region's Tibetan Medical College**, the fourth, and Phur bu don
grub, of the Ngari Mentsikhang (མངའ་རིས་སྨན་རྩིས་ཁང, mnga' ris sman rtsis
khang). The board holds one Kun dga' rig 'dzin only. (The vol. 1 etext
splits the name «ཀུན་ད་གའ»; the scan of vol. 3 reads «ཀུན་དགའ».)

**The contents page**, vol. 3, p. 7 (img. 13), on the scan: the sheets
are the last item but one of the volume, outside its numbered sections,
followed by Mkhyen rab nor bu's digest of WB:

> ཉེར་མཁོའི་དཔེར་བརྗོད་ཁག་ཅིག་གི་ཤོག་ཁྲ།……ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན།(713)
> བེ་དཀར་ལས་འཕྲོད་སྦྱོར་སོགས་ཀྱི་འབྲས་བུ་ཟུར་དུ་བཀོལ་བ།……མཁྱེན་རབ་ནོར་བུ།(730)

**The preface** (སྔོན་འགྲོའི་བརྗོད་གཏམ, sngon 'gro'i brjod gtam) of vol. 3 (etext pp. 17–20,
etext only), signed by Byams pa 'phrin las in the wood pig year of the
17th cycle (1995), names no editor but says how the board was made:
specialists invited from the Mentsikhang's astronomy research institute
and the Mentsikhangs of Shigatse (གཞིས་རྩེ, gzhis rtse), Ngari, Lhokha (ལྷོ་ཁ, lho kha),
Chamdo (ཆབ་མདོ, chab mdo), Nagchu (ནག་ཆུ, nag chu) and Sog county (སོག་རྫོང, sog rdzong), two county hospitals, the Tibetan Medical College and the
Academy of Social Sciences; and of the Chinese reckoning (ནག་རྩིས, nag
rtsis) in this volume, etext p. 20:

> ནག་རྩིས་སྐོར་ལ་མཚོན་ནའང་འབྱུང་རྩིས་མན་ངག་ཟླ་བའི་འོད་ཟེར་གཞིར་བྱས་བཻ་དཀར་ལ་ཁུངས་གཏུགས་ཤིང་། ནག་རྩིས་ཨུཏྤལ་སྔོན་པོའི་དོ་ཤལ་སོགས་ལ་ཟུར་དུ་དཔྱད་དེ་རྡེའུ་འགྲེམས་ལུགས་རྒྱས་འབྲིང་བསྡུས་གསུམ་གྱིས་ལེགས་པར་བརྒྱན་ནས་བཀོད་ཡོད་པ

As for the Chinese reckoning, it is set out on the basis of the
*Moonbeams* (DZ), traced to WB, checked besides against the *Blue Lotus
Garland* of the Chinese reckoning (ཨུཏྤལ་སྔོན་པོའི་དོ་ཤལ, utpal sngon po'i
do shal) and others, and adorned with ways of laying the pebbles in full,
middling and brief. The sheets' title promises the obstacle reckoning
"in full, middling and brief" in the same words: the preface's
description fits them, without naming them or him.

**His other KD writing**, vol. 2, etext pp. 196 and 229 (= pp. 176 and
209), etext only: the heading «ས་བཅད་བཅུ་པ། སྟོང་འཇུག་རི་མོའི་ལག་ལེན་གོ་དོན་དང་བཅས་པའི་རྣམ་བཤད། ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་དང་ཕུར་བུ་དོན་གྲུབ་གཉིས་ཀྱིས་བརྩམས།»
(section 10: an explanation of the practice of the empty-entering's
drawing (རི་མོ, ri mo), with its meaning, written by the two), and its
close:

> ཅེས་སྟོང་འཇུག་རི་མོའི་ལག་ལེན་གོ་དོན་དང་བཅས་པའི་རྣམ་བཤད་འདི་ནི་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་དང་ཕུར་བུ་དོན་གྲུབ་ཀྱིས་པད་དཀར་ཞལ་ལུང་གི་སྟོང་འཇུག་རྒྱས་བཤད་གོ་བདེར་ཚིགས་སུ་བཅད་ནས་ཕྱོགས་བསྒྲིགས་ཞུས་པའོ།། །།

This explanation was compiled by Kun dga' rig 'dzin and Phur bu don grub,
putting the full account of the empty-entering in the *Oral Instruction
of Pundarīka* (པད་དཀར་ཞལ་ལུང, pad dkar zhal lung) into easy verse. No
page of KD gives his dates, birthplace or teachers; vol. 1's appendix
(ཟུར་བཀོད, zur bkod, pp. 648–661) holds short lives of the two chief editors only.

### 3. Outside KD: the same name in the same field

**His article, 1986.** *Collected Articles on Tibetan Medicine and
Reckoning* (བོད་ཀྱི་སྨན་རྩིས་ཆེད་རྩོམ་ཕྱོགས་བསྡུས, bod kyi sman rtsis ched rtsom
phyogs bsdus), "written by the Tibet Autonomous Region's Mentsikhang",
Tibet People's Publishing House (བོད་ལྗོངས་མི་དམངས་དཔེ་སྐྲུན་ཁང, bod ljongs mi
dmangs dpe skrun khang), Lhasa (ལྷ་ས, lha sa) 1986 (BDRC
MW1KG21754; etext IE0OPID02049E0, volume I1KG21849, excellent), pp.
393–421 (etext pp. 405–433, printed = etext − 12 by the contents), etext
only; its title and byline:

> རྒྱ་བོད་རྩིས་ཀྱི་ཉི་ཟླ་འཛིན་ཚུལ་དང་། ལྔ་སྒྲ་གཟའ་ལྔ་སོགས་ས་རིས་དང་འཕྲུལ་འཁོར་བརྩི་ཐབས་ཟུང་འབྲེལ་གྱིས་རྒྱུ་མཚན་འདྲེན་པ་ཕུག་པའི་དགོངས་རྒྱན་ཞེས་བྱ་བ་བཞུགས་སོ།། ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན།

*The Ornament of Phugpa's Intent*, which gives the reasons of the eclipses
of the Chinese and the Tibetan reckonings, the five factors, the five
planets and the rest by the board (ས་རིས, sa ris) and the machine
reckoning (འཕྲུལ་འཁོར, 'phrul 'khor, reckoning by machine) together; by Kun dga' rig 'dzin. It closes on a worked lunar
eclipse timed in Beijing hours, "made clear by machine reckoning
following the *Heart Drop of the Kalkin* (རིགས་ལྡན་སྙིང་ཐིག, rigs ldan snying thig)", and verses of dedication; no
colophon on its writer. A reckoner of the Phugpa (ཕུག་པ, phug pa) school
writing for the Lhasa Mentsikhang's collection, then.

**A teacher of the Mentsikhang sent to observatories.** Byams pa 'phrin
las's memoir, *Memories of Tibetan Medicine* (བྱམས་པ་འཕྲིན་ལས་མཆོག་གི་བོད་ལུགས་གསོ་རིག་གི་དྲན་གསོས,
byams pa 'phrin las mchog gi bod lugs gso rig gi dran gsos; compiled by Tshe
brtan 'jigs med (ཚེ་བརྟན་འཇིགས་མེད, tshe brtan 'jigs med), Nationalities
Publishing House (མི་རིགས་དཔེ་སྐྲུན་ཁང, mi rigs dpe skrun khang), Beijing 2006,
2013; MW00PD1011326; etext IE0OPI156B76B6, search only, `etextchunks`
403), BDRC's search snippet (shown in Wylie, put in script):

> ཕྱིན་ཆད་གནའ་རབས་གནམ་རིག་སྐར་རྩིས་རིག་པ་དང་དེང་རབས་གནམ་དཔྱད་རིག་པ་ཟུང་འབྲེལ་བྱས་ནས་ཚན་རིག་གི་རང་བཞིན་དེ་བས་ཆེ་རུ་འགྲོ་རྒྱུར་ཡིད་ཆེས་དང་འབྲེལ་བྱ་སྤྱོད་ངོ་མའི་ཐོག་ནས་སྔ་ཕྱིར་སོའོ་ཀྲང་གཞོན་པ་ངག་དབང་བཟང་པོ་ལགས་དང་དགེ་རྒན་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་ལགས་སོགས་མི་དྲུག་ནན་ཅིང་ཙེ་ཅིང་ཧྲན་གནམ་རིག་བལྟ་ཁང་དང་པེ་ཅིན་དུ་ཡོད་པའི་ཀྲུང་གོ་ཚན་རིག་ཁང་ཁོངས་རང་བྱུང་ཚན་རིག་ཞིབ་འཇུག་སོའོ་བཅས་པར་སློབ་སྦྱོང་ལ་བཏང་སྟེ

So that, joining the old astronomy and reckoning with modern astronomy,
it might become more scientific, six persons at one time or another,
among them the vice-director Ngag dbang bzang po (ངག་དབང་བཟང་པོ, ngag
dbang bzang po) and the teacher (དགེ་རྒན, dge rgan) Kun dga' rig 'dzin,
were sent to study at the Zijinshan observatory of Nanjing and the
Academy of Sciences' natural-science institute in Beijing. No year in the
snippets read.

**An astronomer of the Lhasa Mentsikhang, a teacher of others.** The
history of medicine and reckoning in Golog (མགོ་ལོག, mgo log), *A Garland of
Jewel Waves* (མགོ་ལོག་སྨན་རྩིས་རིག་པའི་ལོ་རྒྱུས་ནོར་བུའི་རླབས་ཕྲེང, mgo log sman
rtsis rig pa'i lo rgyus nor bu'i rlabs phreng), Qinghai (མཚོ་སྔོན, mtsho sngon)
Nationalities Publishing House, Xining 2000 (MW21932, parts _8D1F54 and _F4C207;
etext IE0OPI4C49C33E, search only), of a Golog physician who made
mercury preparations at Dpal yul (དཔལ་ཡུལ, dpal yul) monastery:

> 1993ལོར་སླར་ཡང་བོད་ལྗོངས་བོད་སྨན་སློབ་གྲྭ་ཆེན་མོའི་དགེ་རྒན་བསམ་གཏན་ལས་རྩ་བཤད་གཉིས་དང་ཕྱི་རྒྱུད་རྩ་ཆུའི་ལེའུ་སོགས་བསླབས། 1996ལོར་ལྷ་ལྡན་སྨན་རྩིས་ཁང་གི་གནམ་རིག་པ་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་ལས་གྲུབ་རྩིས་ལྔ་བསྡུས་གཟའ་ལྔ་རྒྱ་རྩིས་འབྱུང་རྩིས་སོགས་བསླབས།

In 1993 he studied the Root and Explanatory Tantras and the Last Tantra's
chapter on the pulse and urine with Bsam gtan (བསམ་གཏན, bsam gtan), teacher at the Tibet
Autonomous Region's Tibetan Medical College; in 1996 he learned the true
reckoning (གྲུབ་རྩིས, grub rtsis), the five factors, the five planets,
the Chinese reckoning and the elemental reckoning (འབྱུང་རྩིས, 'byung
rtsis) from Kun dga' rig 'dzin, astronomer (གནམ་རིག་པ, gnam rig pa) of
the Lhasa Mentsikhang (ལྷ་ལྡན་སྨན་རྩིས་ཁང, lha ldan sman rtsis khang).
(The years are Arabic numerals in the snippet; the next words run "from
1983 to 1985", so the order is the book's or the OCR's.)

**Teacher of a KD colleague.** Bsod nams dpal ldan (བསོད་ནམས་དཔལ་ལྡན, bsod
nams dpal ldan), on KD's board for the Chamdo Mentsikhang, in his book on
arthritis, China Tibetology Publishing House (ཀྲུང་གོའི་བོད་རིག་པ་དཔེ་སྐྲུན་ཁང,
krung go'i bod rig pa dpe skrun khang), Beijing 2007 (MW1GS88140;
etext IE0OPI9442689C, search only), p. 304:

> རྩིས་རིག་མཁས་དབང་དགེ་རྒན་དྲིན་པོ་ཆེ་རྒན་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་མཆོག་གཙོས་པའི་དགེ་རྒན་དམ་པ་བཅུ་ཕྲག་ལྷག་ཙམ་ལས་གངས་ཅན་སྨན་རྩིས་སྲོལ་རྒྱུན་ཞལ་ནས་ཞལ་དུ་བརྒྱུད་པའི་བཀའ་སློབ་ཅུང་ཟད་ཐོབ

From more than ten holy teachers headed by the expert in reckoning, the
kind teacher, the elder (རྒན, rgan) Kun dga' rig 'dzin, [the author]
received some of the teaching of the Tibetan tradition of medicine and
reckoning handed down from mouth to mouth.

**A national first-grade expert, interviewed.** *Selected Tibetological
Articles from the Journal of Tibet University* (བོད་ལྗོངས་སློབ་གྲྭ་ཆེན་མོའི་རིག་དེབ་ཀྱི་བོད་རིག་པའི་ཞིབ་འཇུག་དཔྱད་རྩོམ་གཅེས་བཏུས,
bod ljongs slob grwa chen mo'i rig deb kyi bod rig pa'i zhib 'jug dpyad rtsom
gces btus), ed. Lhag pa tshe ring (ལྷག་པ་ཚེ་རིང, lhag pa tshe ring),
Nationalities Publishing House, Beijing 2016 (MW1KG25692; etext IE0OPIE8EEAEB9,
search only), a line of its contents:

> གནམ་རིག་སྐར་རྩིས་ཀྱི་འབྲེལ་ཡོད་ཤེས་བྱ་ཕྲན་ཚེགས། སྐར་ནག་རྩིས་ཀྱི་མཁྱེན་པའི་དབང་ཕྱུག་རྒྱལ་ཁབ་རིམ་པའི་རིགས་གཅིག་མཁས་དབང་རྒན་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་ལགས་ལ་བཅར་འདྲི་ཞུས་པའི་ཟིན་ཐོ།

Some knowledge of astronomy and astrology: notes of an interview with
the elder Kun dga' rig 'dzin, master of the astronomical and elemental
reckonings (སྐར་ནག་རྩིས, skar nag rtsis), a national first-grade expert
(རྒྱལ་ཁབ་རིམ་པའི་རིགས་གཅིག་མཁས་དབང, rgyal khab rim pa'i rigs gcig mkhas
dbang). The interview itself was not reached (search only; scans lent on
archive.org, not this reading's).

**Editor and proofreader, later.** The experimental textbooks of Tibetan
medicine for the 21st century (Nationalities Publishing House, Beijing 2004,
revised 2011–2019; MW1KG5258, MW1KG5260, MW1KG5261, MW8LS18526,
MW8LS18538, MW8LS18556, MW8LS68325, MW1PD159451), in their list of the
series, search only: «གནམ་རིག་སྐར་རྩིས། གཙོ་སྒྲིག་པ། བསམ་གྲུབ་རྒྱ་མཚོ། གཙོ་སྒྲིག་གཞོན་པ། ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན།»,
the volume on astronomy and reckoning, chief editor Bsam grub rgya mtsho
(བསམ་གྲུབ་རྒྱ་མཚོ, bsam grub rgya mtsho), deputy chief editor Kun dga' rig
'dzin. The Mentsikhang's almanac for 2013 (Sichuan Nationalities Publishing
House (སི་ཁྲོན་མི་རིགས་དཔེ་སྐྲུན་ཁང, si khron mi rigs dpe skrun khang), Chengdu 2012; MW1PD159386, search only): «ཞུ་དག་པ། ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན། ཡུམ་པ།»,
proofreaders (ཞུ་དག་པ, zhu dag pa) Kun dga' rig 'dzin, Yum pa (ཡུམ་པ, yum pa) and others.

**The Sakya (ས་སྐྱ, sa skya) volume.** In *Reckoning Texts* (རྩིས་གཞུང, rtsis gzhung; the
Sakya *Collected Scriptures* (དཔལ་ལྡན་ས་སྐྱ་པའི་གསུང་རབ, dpal ldan sa skya pa'i gsung
rab) vol. 7, Nationalities Publishing House, Beijing 2004; MW29978), the
supplement to the *Sun's Rays* by Li thang Sde gzhung sprul sku
(ལི་ཐང་སྡེ་གཞུང་སྤྲུལ་སྐུ, li thang sde gzhung sprul sku), *A Needed Supplement to
the Sun's Rays of Astronomy* (སྐར་རྩིས་ཉི་མའི་འོད་ཟེར་གྱི་ཉེར་མཁོའི་ཁ་སྐོང, skar
rtsis nyi ma'i 'od zer gyi nyer mkho'i kha skong; MW29978_8624A4, pp. 223–243), closes, etext I5721
p. 273 (= p. 243), etext only: «དགེའོ། ། སརྦ་མངྒ་ལཾ། ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་གྱིས་རྩིས་མགོ་སྤོས་པ་ཡིན»,
"Kun dga' rig 'dzin moved the epoch": the same work he did on KD's
manuals.

**The national register.** The register of representative transmitters
of national intangible cultural heritage of the People's Republic of
China (ihchina.cn, record 3653, read 2026-10-22), no. 03-1488, 贡嘎仁增
(Gongga Renzeng, the usual Chinese rendering of Kun dga' rig 'dzin), for
the item Ⅹ-121, Tibetan astronomy and calendar reckoning (藏族天文历算),
listed 2008, its keeper the Tibet Autonomous Region's Tibetan Medicine
Hospital. The record states: male, Tibetan, born 1942 in Doilungdêqên
county (堆龙德庆县; སྟོད་ལུང་བདེ་ཆེན, stod lung bde chen; now a district of Lhasa); representative transmitter
of the third batch; of a family of reckoners for generations; a
well-known reckoner and calendar maker of the hospital's institute of
astronomy and calendar reckoning; to the Mentsikhang (门孜康) to learn
astronomy and reckoning in 1952; deputy secretary-general of the
astronomy and calendar society founded in Lhasa in 1981; «《西藏天文历算总汇》一书的主笔之一»,
"one of the main writers of the book *Xizang tianwen lisuan zonghui*";
author of articles, among them one on the relation of Tibetan medicine to
astronomy and reckoning; teacher of more than five hundred farmers' and
herders' students, and at the Tibetan Medical College and Tibet
University.

Not he, by date, place or calling (BDRC's search, 2026-10-22):

  rig 'dzin), at whose request a Sakya colophon in the
  collections of the Path and its Fruit (ལམ་འབྲས, lam 'bras) was written by Sa skya pa Kun dga' blo gros
  (ས་སྐྱ་པ་ཀུན་དགའ་བློ་གྲོས, sa skya pa kun dga' blo gros): «ས་སྐྱ་པ་ཀུན་དགའ་བློ་གྲོས་ལ་སྟག་རྩེ་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་གྱིས་བསྐུལ་དོན་བཞིན་སྦྱར་ཏེ»
  (MW2PD19769, MW23648, MW3PD184; snippets).
- Sa skya pa Kun dga' rig 'dzin (ས་སྐྱ་པ་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན), writer of a
  manuscript ritual in the Sakya Gorum (སྒོ་རུམ, sgo rum) library's

- Kun dga' rig 'dzin of Khri ka (ཁྲི་ཀ, khri ka), in a list of yogins in

  tshogs drug rang grol) autobiography (MW23893_91FEAF).

  monastery before 1959, in a history of Ngari (MW4CZ296089, Dharamsala
  2016).
- A name in a list of Taksham Nuden Dorje's (སྟག་ཤམ་ནུས་ལྡན་རྡོ་རྗེ, stag
  sham nus ldan rdo rje) *Gathered Intent of the Yidam* (ཡི་དམ་དགོངས་འདུས, yi dam
  dgongs 'dus; MW4PD1180, poor OCR).
- BDRC's person records found by the name, none of them he: P4909,
  P5064 (b. 1786), P8842 (b. 1955), P8LS13727, P3JM101 (1897–1959),
  P10MS14669 (1874–1950): other names that hold «kun dga'» and «rig 'dzin».

One mention is doubtful: Tshul khrims rgyal mtshan's life, *Medicine
Nourishing Faith* (དད་པའི་གསོས་སྨན, dad pa'i gsos sman; MW1KG10375_E55D6D, search only), in 1989: «ས་སྦྲུལ (1989)ལོའི་ཟླ་དང་པོའི་ཚེས་བཅོ་ལྔ་ནས་ཕུན་ཚོགས་དབང་མོ་ལ་སྨན་གྱི་རྩ་རྒྱུད་དང་། ཕྱི་རྒྱུད་རྩ་ཆུའི་མདོ་གནང་། ཚེས་བཅོ་བརྒྱད་ནས་འབྱུང་རྩིས་ཟླ་བའི་འོད་ཟེར་ཁྲིད། ཚེས་ཉེར་བཞི་ནས་ཀུན་དགའ་རིག་འཛིན་ལ་ལྔ་པ་འཕྲོད་ཁྲིད།»:
from the fifteenth of the first month of the earth snake year he gave
Phun tshogs dbang mo (ཕུན་ཚོགས་དབང་མོ, phun tshogs dbang mo) the Root Tantra and the Last Tantra's pulse and
urine, from the eighteenth taught the *Moonbeams*, from the twenty-fourth
taught Kun dga' rig 'dzin «ལྔ་པ་འཕྲོད» (unexplained). KD's deputy chief
editor teaching a reckoner of that name fits the writer, but the snippet
does not say who he is.

### Answer

**Answered as far as the sources go.** The writer of the *Sheets of Some
Needed Examples* is the Kun dga' rig 'dzin of KD's own board of editors,
"of the Tibet Autonomous Region's Tibetan Medical College" (vol. 3, img.
5, on the scan): the board holds one man of the name, the sheets are
signed by that name inside the board's own compilation, and his other
KD work is all reckoning (the empty-entering, written with Phur bu don
grub of the Ngari Mentsikhang, and four manuals whose epochs the two
moved to 1987). That much rests on KD alone.

That he is the same man as the Lhasa Mentsikhang's astronomer and
teacher of the 1986 article, the memoir, the Golog history, Bsod nams
dpal ldan's book, the textbook series, the 2013 almanac and the Tibet
University interview, and as the 贡嘎仁增 of the national register (born
1942, at the Mentsikhang from 1952, its hospital's institute of astronomy
and calendar reckoning, teaching at the Medical College and Tibet
University, "one of the main writers" of a Tibetan compendium of
astronomy and calendar reckoning) rests on the name, the field, the
institutions and the period agreeing, and on the epoch-moving that the
Sakya volume and KD both credit to him; no source states the
identification in so many words. No other Kun dga' rig 'dzin of the
period works in reckoning in any source found. BDRC has no person record
for him, so his teachers, students and dates are not in its catalogue;
the register gives only his birth year and county.

### Open

- Open question 249 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): is the *Xizang tianwen lisuan zonghui* (《西藏天文历算总汇》) of
  which the national register makes 贡嘎仁增 "one of the main writers" KD
  itself, whose Chinese title in BDRC's record is *Zang chuan li suan xue
  da quan*? KD's Chinese title page and colophon (lent scans) would settle
  it.
- Open question 250 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): what does the interview with the elder Kun dga' rig 'dzin in
  Tibet University's journal (reprinted in MW1KG25692, Beijing 2016; scans
  lent on archive.org) say of his teachers, his training and the sheets?
- Open question 251 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): when, and for what use, were the sheets drawn: for KD
  (1995–1998), or earlier as teaching material? The year reckoned on the
  obstacle reckoning's sheets is a wood rat (ཤིང་བྱི, shing byi) year;
  no date is written on them.
- Open question 252 ([open-questions.md](open-questions.md)): what is «ལྔ་པ་འཕྲོད» that Tshul khrims rgyal mtshan taught a
  Kun dga' rig 'dzin in 1989, and is he the writer?

## The summing rules left for the moment, the dead and the illness (RD58)

Read 2026-10-27 on BDRC's scans of the 1996 print (vol. 1 I1KG12906,
image = page + 10; vol. 2 I1KG12907, image = page + 8, checked on the
printed numbers of vol. 2 img. 545 «537» and on p. 172's colophon): the
fighting enemies, vol. 2 p. 172 (img. 180, l. 1–21) with the notes p. 537
(img. 545, l. 2 and 17–25) and p. 538 (img. 546, l. 1–5); the dead's twenty
roots, the notes p. 503 (img. 511, l. 1–8) with the end of p. 502 (img.
510); the nine classes of the illness, vol. 1 p. 425 (img. 435) and the
notes p. 496 (img. 504). Yigdzin-1's readings of these images were already
in `wb/yigdzin/` and MITRA's of img. 180, 511 and 545 in `wb/mitra/` (no
new model run); the quoted lines were read by eye on the scan in 3× crops
and the doubtful letters of img. 511 at 8× and 12×. Pages already read
for other sections are named there: the moment's small examinations
([corpus/wb/ch30.md](corpus/wb/ch30.md), pp. 166–172), the notes' worked
example ([corpus/wb/notes-8.md](corpus/wb/notes-8.md)), the nine classes'
rites ([corpus/wb/ch25.md](corpus/wb/ch25.md), p. 425), the dead's roots
([corpus/wb/notes-4.md](corpus/wb/notes-4.md), pp. 499–503). Etext of the
volume and BDRC's search were not used again: every phrase needed was in
the files already read.

The words, named once here: the fighting enemies (འཐབ་དགྲ, 'thab dgra); the
time of reckoning (དུས, dus); the one reckoned (བརྩི་བྱ, brtsi bya); the
life force (སྲོག, srog), the body (ལུས, lus), the power (དབང་ཐང, dbang
thang) and the luck (ཀླུང་རྟ, klung rta); the verdict (ཁ་དམར, kha dmar);
the cutting (གཅོད་འདྲལ, gcod 'dral); rise and decline (དར་གུད, dar gud);
the sky year, the year now running (གནམ་ལོ, gnam lo); the progressed sign
(ལོག་མེན, log men); the nine-eye (དགུ་མིག, dgu mig) and the grave-eye
(དུར་མིག, dur mig); the la, the life-spirit (བླ, bla); the pebble door
(རྡེལ་སྒོ, rdel sgo); speckled (ཁྲ་བོ, khra bo); the deciding pebble
(བཏང་གཅོད, btang gcod).

### 1. The fighting enemies (vol. 2, p. 172, img. 180, l. 20–22; the notes, p. 537, img. 545, l. 2)

> འཐབ་དགྲ་རྣམས་ནི་བརྩི་དུས་ཀྱི། །བརྩི་བྱའི་ལོ་བྱས་བརྩི་བ་ཡིན། །དེ་ཡང་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་འཐབ། །དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས། །འདི་དང་ཆ་མཐུན་གོང་གི་ལེའུ། །ཉེར་བདུན་ནང་དུའང་བཤད་མོད་ཀྱང་། །འདི་ནི་དུས་རྩིས་སྒོས་ཆོས་ཡིན།

The fighting enemies are reckoned by making the time of reckoning the year of
the one reckoned; the life force, body, power and luck fight; count the white
and black and lay the verdict. Its like is told in chapter 27 above, but this
is the time reckoning's own. Directly before it (p. 171, l. 14 to p. 172,
l. 19, img. 179–180) stand the counts it ends: the ogre's seven up and the
demon's seven down from the moment's year, then from fixed years of the sixty
the male counted down (སྦུབ, sbub) and the female "propped" (བཀེན, bken),
fought with the moment (དུས་འཐབ), the cut «ཚུར་བཅད» naming the harm; at the
end «ཡར་བརྒྱད་བགྲངས་པའི་སར། །ཁྱི་འབྲུག་བབས་ན་གནམ་སྒོ་བྱེ», where eight
are counted up it lands on dog or dragon and the sky door opens;
«མར་བརྒྱད་ཕག་སྦྲུལ་ས་སྒོ་བྱེ», eight down on pig or snake, the earth door;
«ས་ནས་ཡར་གསུམ་བགྲངས་པའི་སར། །བྱ་སྤྲེལ་བྱུང་ན་བྱེ་བྲལ་བབས», three up from
the earth, on bird or monkey, parting; «ས་ནས་མར་གསུམ་གླང་ལུག་བབས», three
down, ox or sheep, quarrels and lawsuits (the whole in
[corpus/wb/ch30.md](corpus/wb/ch30.md), *wb.2:p171-172*, and
[claims/moment-sixty-pairs.md](claims/moment-sixty-pairs.md)).

The notes, p. 537, l. 2, the whole sentence, closing the reckoning of the
moment («དུས་རྩིས་སོ»):

> ཡར་གསུམ་སོགས་ལ་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་བཞིས་འཐབ། དུས་ཀྱིས་བརྩི་བྱའི་ལོ་ཚབ་བྱའོ།

For the three up and the rest, the four, life force, body, power and luck,
fight; the time takes the place of the year of the one reckoned.

What the scan gives:

- **«ཡར་གསུམ་སོགས» is the "three up" of p. 172's own counts**, "and the
  rest" the three down, eight up, eight down, and with them seven up and
  down: those are the only «ཡར»/«མར» counts on pp. 171–172. This settles the
  doubt of ROADMAP RD58's row, which also read it as ch. 26's board of
  three (vol. 1, p. 471, «ཡར་གསུམ», [claims/thirty-check-order-yar-mar.md](claims/thirty-check-order-yar-mar.md)):
  the same words stand there for the board's upper three; that the notes
  mean the board is not excluded by the wording, but the notes' sentence
  stands under the counts it follows and p. 172 names ch. 27, not ch. 26, as
  its like (see Open).
- **Which years count:** the years landed on by those counts, from the
  signs they start at: for the ogre and the demon the moment's year itself;
  for the sixty-year pairs the fixed years the lines name (iron male dog,
  earth female sheep, and so on), male counted down and female up; and the
  eight and three counts from iron dragon, water pig, fire ox and wood ox.
  The landed year is what "fights" and is "cut inward" or not.
- **Which aspects fight which:** the four, life force, body, power, luck,
  and only these four (not the mewa and not the la, which chapter 27's
  circles add as a fifth, [claims/circles-count.md](claims/circles-count.md));
  the verse says "fight" and does not say which of the two years' aspects
  meet. By the like in ch. 26 and 27, which the verse names, an aspect meets
  the same aspect of the other year (claim [thirty-eight-fighters](claims/thirty-eight-fighters.md),
  question 97; [circles-count](claims/circles-count.md)): the life force the
  life force, body body, power power, luck luck. The colours would be ch.
  27's (mother and friend, earth with earth and water with water white; son,
  enemy, wood, iron and fire with their own black, [claims/circles-colour-rule.md](claims/circles-colour-rule.md)),
  but the notes on the moment (p. 536) report another usage for ch. 30
  (son and the inner meetings of earth and water speckled): not said for
  this verse.
- **The time in place of the one reckoned's year:** the notes' «ལོ་ཚབ» "takes
  the place of the year". Two readings fit the words and the pool does not
  choose between them: (a) the moment's year stands where ch. 26 and 27
  put the person's own, so the moment's four aspects are the ones that
  fight the landed year (the counts' lines say «དུས་འཐབ», "fights with the
  moment"); (b) only the starting sign is taken from the moment, and the
  asker's own aspects fight. The notes' worked example, which follows at
  once (p. 537, l. 19 to p. 538, l. 4, [corpus/wb/notes-8.md](corpus/wb/notes-8.md),
  *wb.2:p537-538*), reads as (b): the sky year is the wood ox of the east
  season, the one reckoned a water rat of fourteen; "the life force's enemy
  is sought": the sky year's wood has earth for its enemy, so earth is
  needed; the one reckoned is a male year, so a male year; of the earth
  years the dog agrees in life force and power; from the earth dog the years
  are counted down by his fourteen and fall on the iron pig, and the one
  reckoned's life force fights it, «ཆུ་ནང་ཕྲད», a water inner meeting. The
  count checks on the sixty (earth dog the 35th, iron pig the 48th:
  thirteen steps on, the first place being the starting year, so fourteen
  places), and the body and power the same from the wood tiger (the 51st)
  to the fire hare (the 4th: thirteen steps over the sixty). The sixty-year
  pairs' lines say "fought with the moment"; how this and the example fit
  is the open question below.
- **How the count is laid:** the example fixes it for a male: the starting
  year, counted down (སྦུབ) through the number of his years lived, the
  starting year the first (iron pig is thirteen on from earth dog, for
  fourteen); a female is "propped" (བཀེན, bken) in the verses, which sets
  her the other way as ch. 26 has it (a female counted up); no line of ch.
  30 states how many years the pairs' count runs. The same example finds
  the la as the life force's mother (here iron) from the earth dog (the
  "fourth executioner") to the iron pig, and the own grave of the water rat
  (the water dragon) and the executioner's grave (the earth dragon) by the
  dog, so that these are fought as the life force; and it closes on "the
  three hundred and sixty starting points": «སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་དབང་བླ་རང་དུར་གཤེད་དུར་བཅས་དྲུག་གིས་འཐབ་ཟླ་ལོ་དྲུག་ཅུས་བསྐེས་པས་སུམ་བརྒྱ་དྲུག་ཅུ»,
  six that fight times the sixty make 360 (img. 546, l. 2–4; the line lists
  eight words, «སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་དབང་བླ་རང་དུར་གཤེད་དུར», for its six; read
  so on the scan at 5×, the second «དབང» as printed).
- **The verdict:** «དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས», count white and black and lay
  the verdict: the larger colour decides, as in ch. 27 and 26; no line of
  p. 172 says what a tie reads or sizes the verdict.

### 2. The place's three: cutting, rise and decline, verdict (pp. 166–170; question 423)

pp. 166–168 were read on the scan for RD40 ([corpus/wb/ch30.md](corpus/wb/ch30.md),
*wb.2:p166*, *wb.2:p167-168*) and the lines below were set again beside
img. 174 (p. 166) at 3×. The root (p. 166): «བྱ་ར་དང་ནི་བྱེད་ཡོར་གྱི། །འབྱུང་བ་རྣམ་པ་ལྔ་པོ་ལ། །རེ་རེ་ལའང་གཅོད་འདྲལ་དང་། །དར་གུད་ཁ་དམར་གསུམ་གསུམ་ཡོད། །རེ་རེ་ལའང་ཁང་ས་དང་། །ཞིང་ས་དུར་ས་རང་ས་དང་། །ཉེ་བའི་ས་དང་ལོངས་སྤྱོད་ས། །ནད་ས་གདོན་ས་བརྒྱད་བརྒྱད་ཡོད།»:
for each of the five elements of the moment (བྱ་ར, bya ra) and of the one
reckoned (བྱེད་ཡོར, byed yor) there are three, the cutting (གཅོད་འདྲལ),
rise and decline and the verdict, and for each eight places: house, field,
grave, one's own place, the near one's, enjoyment's, illness's and the
demon's. The head of the passage says how they are made: «དེ་ཡི་འབྱུང་བ་ངོས་བཟུང་ལ། །གང་ཡིན་དེ་ཡི་ཁ་སྨྲང་བཏགས», find the element of
it and lay its verdict (ཁ་སྨྲང, kha smrang).

What the pages show of how the three make a place's verdict:

- **The cutting** is the five relations of the asker's aspect to the moment's
  element: the notes, p. 535–536 (img. 543, read for RD40; corpus/wb/notes-8.md):
  «ཁང་ས་བརྩི་ན་འདྲི་དུས་ཀྱི་སྲོག་གིས་བརྩི་བྱའི་སྲོག་བཅད་པ་སྟེ་དགྲར་གྱུར་ན་ཞེས་སོགས་གཞུང་བཞིན། ཕར་བཅད་ན་གྲོགས་ཕྲད། ཚུར་བསྐྱེད་མ། ཕར་བསྐྱེད་བུ། ནང་ཕྲད་སོ་སོའི་བཤད་པ་གཞུང་བཞིན།»:
  the moment's life force cutting the one reckoned's is the enemy; cutting
  outward, a friend meets; generating inward, a mother; generating outward, a
  son; the same element, an inner meeting, each as the text tells. This is
  the house's list (p. 166): moment cuts the asker, the asker cuts the
  moment, each generates the other, the same; and each of the five elements
  has its own reading for the inner meeting. So the cutting gives the
  place's reading by relation, and by element where the elements meet.
- **Rise and decline** gives presence or absence, not a second reading of
  the cutting: the field, «བརྩི་བྱའི་དབང་ཐང་གང་ཡིན་དེ། །དུས་ཚོད་སྟེང་དུ་དར་གུད་སྦྱར། །དར་ཡོད་གུད་ན་མེད་པར་དཔྱད», the one reckoned's power, whichever it is,
  put on the moment's element for rise and decline: rising, there is a
  field, declining, none (p. 167, img. 175); the grave, «ཀླུང་རྟའི་དར་གུད་དུས་སྟེངས་ཁྱེར། །གུད་ན་ལམ་གྲོག་ས་དགྲ་ཡོད», the luck's rise and decline
  carried on the moment: declining, a road, ravine or ground enemy (p.
  168); the own place, «དར་གུད་བཟང་དྲུག་ཤར་ན་ཡོད། །ངན་པ་དྲུག་ལ་མེད་པར་ཤོད», the good six of rise and decline, if in the east, there
  is [the kin or enemy], the bad six, none (p. 168); wealth (p. 168, l. 4 of
  img. 176): «བརྩི་བྱའི་ལུས་དང་དབང་ཐང་གཉིས། །དུས་ཚོད་དང་སྦྱར་དར་གུད་བརྩི། །དར་ཡོད་གུད་ན་མེད་ལུང་སྟོན། །བྱ་རས་ལུས་དབང་གཅོད་ཀྱང་མེད། །དེ་གཉིས་བསྟུན་ལ་ཁ་དམར་གདགས»:
  body and power put on the moment for rise and decline, rising there is,
  declining the prophecy is none; and if the moment cuts body and power,
  none also; where these two agree, lay the verdict.
- **The verdict** is therefore laid on what the cutting and the rise and
  decline agree on: the line of wealth says it outright («དེ་གཉིས་བསྟུན་ལ»,
  where the two accord); for the other places the two are read side by
  side in the place's own lines and the verdict is named only at p. 166's
  head. The six good and six bad of rise and decline are the twelve stages
  of the chapters on the dead and the year ([claims/dead-living-twenty-one.md](claims/dead-living-twenty-one.md):
  the good six white, the bad six black). No line of pp. 166–170 counts
  white and black for a place, as p. 172 does for the fighting enemies, and
  none ranks the three above one another.
- Question 423's items, as the lines give them: **wealth** there when the
  asker's body and power both rise on the moment and the moment cuts
  neither (the two agreeing: «བསྟུན»); **beer** (p. 169) likewise body and
  power both; **the sick one's life** by rise, decline and cutting (p. 169),
  rising no death, declining the cutting also reckoned, in between a long
  illness; **trade** and disputes (p. 170) by two aspects (power with luck)
  named for both; the casting's grain (the notes, p. 536) by the moment's life
  force against the asker's power. All of these are read from the pages
  already in [claims/moment-wealth-sick-demon.md](claims/moment-wealth-sick-demon.md);
  nothing on these pages shows any of them read otherwise.

### 3. The dead's twenty roots (the notes, vol. 2, p. 503, img. 511, l. 1–8; the end of p. 502, img. 510)

The end of p. 502 (img. 510, last six lines, Yigdzin-1, the scan not
re-read there): the dead's time of death by the first examination's
pebbles, «ལོ་འགྲོས་འཆི་དུས་བཅུ་གཉིས་ནི། རང་ཀེག བདུན་ཟུར། མཐུན་གསུམ། བཞི་གཤེད། ཁྱིམ་མཚེས། རྒྱ་མདའ་ཐུག་པ། སྲི་ངན། གསང་ཀེག ལོ་སྐར་ཆེ་བ། ཆུང་བ། ལོག་མིན་བདུད་གཅོད། བླ་འཁྱམས་བཅས་སོ། །དེ་ཡང་ལོ་ཟླ་ཞག་དུས་ལ་བརྟག་ཅེས་འདུག་གོ། །དར་གུད་ནི་སྲོག་སོགས་བཞི་གནམ་ལོར་གཏུགས་པའི་བཟང་དྲུག་དཀར་པོ་ངན་དྲུག་ནག། དགུ་མིག་རྐྱང་པ་དང་། གཤེད་འདོམས་སྦྲགས་མ་ཀེག་རྩིས་བཞིན་དང་། དུར་མིག་ཚུད་པ་ལ་གཤིན་པོའི་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་བླ་ལྔ་རང་མ་བུ་གྲོགས་དགྲ་ལྔར་ཕྱེ་ནས་སོ་སོའི་དགུ་མིག་ཡིན་མིན་ལྔ་སྟེ་སྲོག་གི་རང་མ་སོགས་ལྔ་ཀའི་བཏང་གཅོད་གཅིག་ཏུ་བསྡུ་སོགས་འགྲེ། སྤར་ཁའི་དགུ་མིག་ཕོ་ལི་མོ་ཁམ་ལ་ཐུག་མིན། སྨེ་བའི་དབུས་བབ་དང་བྱིས་པ་གཅིག་དཀར་ལ་བབ་སོགས་ཡོད་མེད། རུས་ཁམས་རང་མ་བུ་གྲོགས་དགྲ་ལྔའི་དུར་མིག་ཡིན་མིན་ལས་བཏང་།»
(the sentence runs over to p. 503): the twelve "times of death by the
course of the years": one's own obstacle, the seventh, the triad, the four
executioners, the house-and-kin («ཁྱིམ་མཚེས»), meeting the Chinese arrow,
the bad sri, the secret obstacle, the year's mansion great and small, the
progressed sign's demon cut, and the la's wandering, examined on the year,
month, day and hour; rise and decline: the life force and the other three
set against the sky year, the six good white, the six bad black; the
nine-eye of the single, and the executioner's and the joined ones as in the
obstacle reckoning; for the grave-eye falling in, the dead's life force,
body, power, luck and la, each divided into five (own, mother, son,
friend, enemy), whether each of the five falls in its nine-eye, five, the
life force's own, mother and the rest of all five gathered into one
deciding pebble; the trigram's nine-eye, whether it meets the male, the
*li*, the female or the *kham*; the mewa, whether it falls in the centre
or on the child's one white, and so on, there or not; the clan element's
five (own, mother, son, friend, enemy) whether in the grave-eye, from
which the deciding pebble [is taken].

Then p. 503, l. 1–8, read on the scan:

> གཅོད་བཞག ཟླ་ཞག་དུས་གསུམ། རང(༡) བདུན(༢) མཐུན(༣) བཞི(༤) ལོ་སྐར་ཆེ(༥) ཆུང(༦) བཅས་ཐུག་མིན་དང་བཅས་བརྟག དེ་རྣམས་ལས་སྤར་ཁའི་འཆི་དུས་ལྔ་ལྟ་བུ་ལས་གང་རུང་བྱུང་ན་འཆི་དུས་ཡིན་པས་རྡེའུ་ནག་པ་ལྟ་བུ་ཀུན་ལ་འགྲེ། དེ་ལྟར་སྤར་སྨེ་ལོ་གསུམ་དང་སྲོག་སོགས་དར་གུད་བཞི། རྐྱང་པའི་དགུ་མིག་སྦྲགས་མིའི་དགུ་མིག་གཉིས་དང་། སྲོག་དུར་ཞུགས་སོགས་བླ་བར་ལྔ་དང་། སྤར་ཁ་དང་སྨེ་བའི་དགུ་མིག་གཉིས། རུས་ཁམས་ཀྱི་གཅིག ལོ་ཟླ་ཞག་གསུམ་གྱི་གསུམ་སྟེ་ཉི་ཤུ་ཐམ་པ་དང་། ཉི་ཤུ་པོ་དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས་པའི་སྤྱིའི་ཁ་དམར་རྡེལ་གཅིག་སྟེ། བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཉེར་གཅིག་ལ་འཆི་དུས་རྡེལ་ཕྲན་ཉེར་གཅིག་ཏུ་འཇོག་པ་འདྲ།

The first line, set: the month, day and time, three: [the six marked] 1 own,
2 seventh, 3 triad, 4 four [executioners], 5 the year's mansion great and 6
small, with the not-meeting, are examined; if any of these like the
trigram's five times of death arises, it is the time of death, laid
as a black pebble, and so for all. So, **the twenty**: the trigram, mewa and
year, three; the rise and decline of the life force and the other three,
four; the single's nine-eye and the joined man's nine-eye, two; the life
force's grave-entering and the rest, up to the la, five; the nine-eyes of
the trigram and the mewa, two; the clan element's one; the three of the
three, year, month and day: twenty in all (3+4+2+5+2+1+3 = 20); counting
the white and black of the twenty, the general verdict, one pebble: the
deciding pebble's twenty-one are laid as twenty-one small pebbles of the
time of death, "as it seems". Lines 6–8 (from «ཉི་ཤུ་པོ») were read from
Yigdzin-1 and MITRA, which agree but for «ཁྲ་མར» (Yigdzin-1) and «ཁྭ་མར»
(MITRA) at the sentence's end, «བུ་དཔེའི་ཁྲ་མར་དེ་ལྟར་འདུག»: not understood,
not read on the scan.

Reading, letter by letter: «བླ་བར» in the fifth group is «བླ་བར» in Yigdzin-1
and «ཟླ་བར» in MITRA; on the scan (img. 511, l. 4, 8× and 12×) the stacked
letter is the same shape as the «ཟླ» of l. 1 («ཟླ་ཞག») and as the «བླ» of
l. 19 («བླ་ཁྱེར»); the print does not tell them apart at this size. The sense
decides: the sentence of p. 502's end names the five as «སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་བླ་ལྔ»
(life force, body, power, luck, la), so «བླ་བར་ལྔ» is "up to the la, five"
and «ཟླ་བར», "in the month", would leave the group of five without its fifth;
the count of twenty holds with either. The fifth line's «རུས་ཁམས» is read so
(the clan element, the same word as p. 500's «རུས་ཁམས་རང་མ་བུ་གྲོགས་དགྲ»),
MITRA's «དུས་ཁམས» is its own.

What the twenty are, as the passages give them, against the roots of the
first examination: pp. 45–46 and the notes, p. 500, name roots of the dead's
pebbles (the year of death, the progressed sign, trigram, mewa, rise and decline, month, day, hour),
four aspects fought on each (RD16, RD23, [reckonings.md](reckonings.md));
p. 503's twenty are another cut of the same examination: seven groups
counted as pebbles (the trigram, mewa and year; the four rises and declines;
two nine-eyes; five grave-eyes by aspect; two nine-eyes of trigram and mewa;
the clan element; the three of year, month and day), and the twelve times
of death by the course of the years (the end of p. 502) stand beside them as
the tests of the time, not as pebbles of the twenty. How the notes lay the
twenty and the twelve together («ཉི་ཤུ་པོ་དཀར་ནག་བགྲངས»: the twenty counted
white and black) is as a pebble of the general verdict, the twenty-first,
which the notes call «བཏང་གཅོད་རྡེའུ་ཉེར་གཅིག», the deciding pebble's twenty-one.

### 4. The illness's nine classes from the fifteen (vol. 1, p. 425, img. 435; the notes, p. 496, img. 504, l. 2–9)

The nine classes of p. 425 read on the scan for RD22 and RD41 and
[corpus/wb/ch25.md](corpus/wb/ch25.md) (*wb.1:p425*) gives them whole with
their rites; the 1996 print's Yigdzin-1 drops the line of the fifth black
(«ལྔ་ཆས་ནག»); the reprint has it. In WB's order: a third more white, a
fourth, a fifth, a sixth more white, white and black half, a sixth, a
fifth, a fourth, a third more black. The notes, p. 496 (img. 504, l. 2–9; the
sentence read on the Gser thang reprint for RD53):

> རྡེལ་སྒོ་མ་བརྩིས་པའི་བཅོ་ལྔ་ལས་དྲུག་དཀར་དགུ་ནག་ན་སུམ་ཆས་ལྷག་ནག དེ་ལྡོག་གསུམ་ཆའི་ལྷག་དཀར་དང་། ལྔ་དཀར་གཞན་ནག་ན་ལྔ་ཆས་ལྷག་ནག་སོགས་འགྲེ། དཀར་ནག་སྙོམས་ཁར་དཀར་བཞི་ལྷག་ན་བཞི་ཆས་ལྷག་སོགས་སྦྱོར་དགོས་ཏེ། གཞན་དུ་ལྔ་ནག་བཅུ་དཀར་ན་སུམ་ཆས་ལྷག་དཀར་སོགས་སྦྱར་དགོས་ནའང་དེ་ལྟར་བརྩིས་མི་འདུག་པས་སོ་ཟེར་ཀྱང་། དོན་ལ་དཀར་པོ་གསུམ་མང་ན་སུམ་ཆ་བཞི་བཞི་ཆ་སོགས་དང་འདྲ་བར་འདུག

Of the fifteen without the pebble door: six white and nine black, a third more
black; the reverse, a third more white; five white and the rest black, a fifth
more black, and so on. Over an even split of white and black, four more white,
a fourth more, and the rest must be joined; otherwise five black and ten white
would have to be joined as a third more white, which is not counted so, they
say; but in fact three more white is like a third, four more like a fourth.

How the class follows from the fifteen, as the passages give it (claim
[illness-classes-by-difference](claims/illness-classes-by-difference.md), RD53):
count the white and the black of the fifteen, the pebble door left out; the
class is named by how many more of one colour there are: three more, «སུམ་ཆས»;
four, «བཞི་ཆས»; five, «ལྔ་ཆས»; six, «དྲུག་ཆས»; the two colours even,
«དཀར་ནག་ཕྱེད་མ». The notes' two examples, six against nine and five against
ten, are the differences three and five; they do not give the third's
fraction. What this reading adds, arithmetic on those words and not a line of
the notes: the fifteen are laid in three rows of five (the notes, p. 495, img. 503, Yigdzin-1 only,
«རྡེལ་སྒོ་སྤངས་པའི་བཅོ་ལྔ་པོ་ཚོ་གསུམ་བྱས་པ་གཅིག་ནས་ལྔ་བར་དང་པོ་སོགས»), and the
notes say the speckled is counted neither "outer" nor "inner" in the reading
of the houses (p. 495, img. 503, Yigdzin-1 only, «ཁྲ་བོ་ནི་ཕྱི་ནང་གང་དུའང་བགྲང་བ་མེད་ཀྱང་བྱུང་ན་ཅུང་ཞན»); with
white and black alone the difference of fifteen is always odd (three, five),
so WB's even classes, "four more", "six more" and "even", can arise only if
some of the fifteen are speckled and belong to neither colour; the classes
cover differences 0, 3, 4, 5 and 6 on either side and say nothing of 1, 2
and seven or more.

The order on the page is not one of rising white: the white classes stand as
three, four, five, six more, then half, then the black as six, five, four,
three more: a fold at the half. The labels at the ends of the rites are in
[claims/illness-class-labels.md](claims/illness-class-labels.md) (RD53); no
new reading of them.

### Open

- Question: the notes' «ཡར་གསུམ་སོགས་ལ་སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་བཞིས་འཐབ། དུས་ཀྱིས་བརྩི་བྱའི་ལོ་ཚབ་བྱའོ» (vol. 2, p. 537): are «ཡར་གསུམ་སོགས» the three up, three down, eight up and eight down of p. 172's own counts (img. 180, l. 16–19), whose landed years the four aspects fight; and does «དུས་ཀྱིས་བརྩི་བྱའི་ལོ་ཚབ་བྱ» put the moment's year, with its four aspects, where the asker's is, so that the moment's aspects fight the landed year (the lines' «དུས་འཐབ»), or does the moment only give the element the starting sign is sought by, as the notes' worked example (p. 537, the water rat of fourteen and the wood ox's sky year) has it, the asker's own aspects fighting?
- Question: how many years does p. 171–172's count of the sixty-year pairs and of the ogre's and demon's seven up and down run (the asker's years lived, as in the worked example, or one place), and is a female counted up as in ch. 26 («བཀེན» "propped")?
- Question: in the notes' close on the 360 starting points (vol. 2, p. 538, img. 546, l. 2–4) the six that fight are written as «སྲོག་ལུས་དབང་ཀླུང་དབང་བླ་རང་དུར་གཤེད་དུར»: eight words; which six are meant, and is the second «དབང» a slip, the luck among them though the worked example does not use it?
- Question: p. 166's «གཅོད་འདྲལ། དར་གུད། ཁ་དམར» three to each element: is the verdict (ཁ་དམར) of a place laid, as p. 168 says of wealth, where the cutting and the rise and decline agree (white, a good place; black, a bad), and is there a white-and-black count for each of the eight places, as p. 172 says of the fighting enemies, which the lines of pp. 166–168 do not give?
- Question: in the nine classes (vol. 1, p. 425; the notes, p. 496), what does a difference of one, two, or seven and more of the fifteen read, and is the speckled counted in the fifteen but in neither colour (an arithmetic inference), so that "four more" and "six more" arise?
- Question: in p. 503's twenty, whose are «ལོ་» and the three of the group «ལོ་ཟླ་ཞག་གསུམ་གྱི་གསུམ»: the year, month and day of death with the twelve times of death's tests, or the dead's own year; and is the group's «བླ་བར» (in the notes' sentence the la, up to which the five are counted) as it is read here?

### Searched and not found

- 2026-10-27, the notes' pp. 503 and 537 (img. 511, 545, 546) and vol. 2 p. 172 (img. 180) on the scan: no sentence says which aspect of the landed year each of the four fights, nor how many years the sixty-year pairs' count runs; ch. 27 «ཉེར་བདུན» is named as the like, but its circles count the asker's years lived upward, with five aspects (circles-count).
- 2026-10-27, vol. 2 pp. 166–168 (img. 174–176, the lines of the three) and the notes' p. 536: no line says that a place's verdict is a count of white and black, or how the cutting and rise and decline are ranked.
- 2026-10-27, vol. 1 p. 425 and the notes p. 496 (img. 435, 504): no class for a difference of one, two or seven and more; no statement whether the speckled counts to the fifteen.
- 2026-10-27, p. 503 img. 511: the print does not tell «བླ» from «ཟླ» in the fifth group; no other print of the notes (Mipham's own, I2029, or the Chengdu Collected Works) was opened for the letter.
